1. MDA State Hemp Program: structure and legal basis

1.1 Who runs it, and under what law

Hemp in Montana is regulated by the Montana Department of Agriculture (MDA), not by the Department of Revenue Cannabis Control Division (CCD/CARD), which regulates marijuana. MDA's hemp page states, verbatim in substance (opened 1 Nov 2026, https://agr.mt.gov/hemp):

"A hemp license is required to cultivate hemp in Montana, applicants must comply with Section 80-18-101 through 80-18-111 of MCA and ARM 4.19.101-202. A hemp license provides authorization for the production of hemp at a particular growing area by a particular individual or entity."

The same page states:

"Montana is operating its 2026 State Hemp Program as a USDA approved Plan, effective January 27, 2023. Potential licensees are encouraged to review this plan to be aware of and understand the requirements and procedures related to licensure, sampling, testing for Total THC, reporting, remediation options, and enforcement."

1.2 The Chapter 18 vs Chapter 27 conflict — RESOLVED in favour of Chapter 18

Run 1 cited hemp statutes as Title 80, Chapter 18 (§§80-18-101–111). The Parts 9–10 track cited Title 80, Chapter 27, "Montana Hemp Program". This round resolves the conflict for assembly purposes as follows:

  • The official MCA 2025 index page for Title 80, Chapter 18, Part 1, opened directly (https://leg.mt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/sections_index.html, accessed 1 Nov 2026), is headed "TITLE 80. AGRICULTURE — CHAPTER 18. ALTERNATIVE AGRICULTURAL CROPS — Part 1. Hemp" and lists §§80-18-101 (Definitions), 80-18-102 (Hemp authorized as agricultural crop), 80-18-103 (Hemp — licensing), 80-18-106 (Hemp production — notification requirements), 80-18-107 (Rulemaking authority), 80-18-110 (Disposition of fees), and 80-18-111 (Affirmative defense for possession or cultivation of marijuana), with §§80-18-104/105 and 80-18-108/109 reserved.
  • MDA's hemp page, its licensing pages, its processor page, its 2026 Stage 1 application form, and the State Hemp Plan all cite the same range: §§80-18-101 through 80-18-111, MCA, and ARM 4.19.101–202.
  • The State Hemp Plan's authority section (§1, p. 2, document dated 3 Jan 2023, https://agr.mt.gov/_docs/industrialhemp-docs/MT-State-Hemp-Plan.pdf, accessed 1 Nov 2026) states: "Title 80, Chapter 18, Sections 101–111, Montana Code Annotated (MCA), the Administrative Rules of Montana (ARM), Chapter 4.19.101–202, and the Agriculture Improvement Act (Farm Bill) of 2018."

Assembly instruction: use Title 80, Chapter 18 throughout. The Chapter 27 citation was not corroborated by any MDA or MCA source opened in this round; no Title 80, Chapter 27 hemp programme was located in those sources. That non-corroboration is recorded in §11 as a Verify item (whether Chapter 27 exists for some other subject), but it must not be carried into the final file as the hemp chapter.

1.3 The MCA sections, verbatim operative text (MCA 2025, opened 1 Nov 2026)

§80-18-101. Definitions — https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/section_0010/0800-0180-0010-0010.html. History: En. Sec. 1, Ch. 360, L. 2001; amd. Sec. 1, Ch. 289, L. 2019; amd. Sec. 1, Ch. 59, L. 2021; amd. Sec. 12, Ch. 746, L. 2023 (HB 948):

  • (1)(a) "'Hemp' means the plant species Cannabis sativa L. and any part of that plant, including the seeds and all derivatives, extracts, cannabinoids, isomers, acids, salts, and salts of isomers, whether growing or not, with a total delta-9 tetrahydrocannabinol concentration of not more than 0.3% on a dry weight basis."
  • (1)(b) "The term does not include synthetic cannabinoids."
  • (2) "'Hemp crude' means a hemp derivative in a temporary state of not complying with the legal definition of hemp, the amount of tetrahydrocannabinol, or the amount of tetrahydrocannabinolic acid that will be further processed in order to comply."
  • (3) "'Hemp derivatives' means all products that contain or are processed from, extracted from, or manufactured from hemp."
  • (4) "'Marijuana' means all plant material from the genus Cannabis containing tetrahydrocannabinol (THC) or seeds of the genus capable of germination." [Note: this §80-18-101(4) definition is a hemp-statute definition; the operative marijuana definition for Title 16, Chapter 12 is §16-12-102, which excludes hemp as provided in §80-18-101. Do not quote (4) as the Chapter 12 definition.]
  • (5) "'Synthetic cannabinoids' has the meaning provided in 50-32-222 and includes any cannabinoids produced artificially, whether from chemical synthesis or biosynthesis using recombinant biological agents, including but not limited to yeast and algae."

§80-18-102. Hemp authorized as agricultural crop — https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/section_0020/0800-0180-0010-0020.html. History: En. Sec. 2, Ch. 360, L. 2001; amd. Sec. 2, Ch. 289, L. 2019; amd. Sec. 2, Ch. 59, L. 2021:

"Hemp is considered an agricultural crop in this state. Upon meeting the requirements of 80-18-103, an individual in this state may plant, grow, harvest, possess, process, sell, or buy hemp, including cultivatable seeds or plants."

§80-18-103. Hemp — licensing — https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/section_0030/0800-0180-0010-0030.html. History: En. Sec. 3, Ch. 360, L. 2001; amd. Sec. 3, Ch. 289, L. 2019; amd. Sec. 3, Ch. 59, L. 2021:

  • (1) "An individual growing hemp or selling propagatable hemp or hemp seeds shall apply to the department for a license on a form prescribed by the department."
  • (2) "The application for a license must include the name and address of the applicant and the legal description of the land area to be used for the production of hemp and any additional information required by rule."
  • (3) "The licensing requirements of this part do not apply to employees of the agricultural experiment station or the Montana state university-Bozeman extension service involved in research and extension-related activities."

§80-18-106. Hemp production — notification requirements — https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/section_0060/0800-0180-0010-0060.html. History: En. Sec. 4, Ch. 360, L. 2001; amd. Sec. 4, Ch. 289, L. 2019; amd. Sec. 4, Ch. 59, L. 2021:

"Each licensee shall file with the department: (1) documentation showing the type and variety of seeds; and (2) a copy of any contract to grow hemp."

§80-18-107. Rulemaking authority — https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/section_0070/0800-0180-0010-0070.html. History: En. Sec. 5, Ch. 360, L. 2001; amd. Sec. 5, Ch. 289, L. 2019; amd. Sec. 5, Ch. 59, L. 2021. "The department shall adopt rules that include but are not limited to: (1) testing of the hemp during growth to determine tetrahydrocannabinol levels; (2) supervision of the hemp during its growth and harvest; (3) assessment of a fee that is commensurate with the costs of the department's activities in licensing, testing, and supervising hemp production; (4) a hemp definition that complies with United States department of agriculture requirements and state regulation; (5) regulations for hemp derivatives and hemp crude, including licensing, fees, and transportation requirements; (6) necessary licensing requirements; and (7) any other rules and procedures necessary to carry out this part."

§80-18-110. Disposition of fees — https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/section_0100/0800-0180-0010-0100.html. History: En. Sec. 6, Ch. 360, L. 2001:

"All fees assessed as provided in 80-18-107 must be deposited with the state treasurer to the credit of the state special revenue fund for the use of the department for administering and enforcing this part."

The State Hemp Plan (§7) states the same in operational terms: fees must be commensurate with operating the programme, are deposited to the state special revenue fund for MDA's administering/enforcing the programme, and the Legislature does not provide additional funding for the programme's operation; fees do not currently cover all programme costs.

§80-18-111. Affirmative defense for possession or cultivation of marijuana — https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0800/chapter_0180/part_0010/section_0110/0800-0180-0010-0110.html. History: En. Sec. 7, Ch. 360, L. 2001; amd. Sec. 6, Ch. 289, L. 2019; amd. Sec. 6, Ch. 59, L. 2021:

"It is an affirmative defense to a prosecution for the possession or cultivation of marijuana that the defendant was growing or processing hemp in compliance with this part or a tribal plan approved by the United States department of agriculture."

1.4 USDA-approved plan: three dates that must not be conflated

  1. Federal final rule effective 22 March 2021. The USDA AMS plan table (opened in full, https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/state-and-tribal-plan-review, updated 17 Feb 2026, accessed 1 Nov 2026) defines "Approved " as "Plan approved under the final rule which took effect March 22, 2021", and lists Montana as "MT Approved Plan (pdf)".
  2. Original Montana plan approval announced 6 March 2020 (USDA announcement, secondary via Constant Contact republication, accessed 1 Nov 2026): USDA announced approval of hemp production plans for Georgia, Montana, and the Sisseton-Wahpeton Oyate Tribe. That is the original approval, not the current plan version.
  3. Current plan document dated 3 January 2023, version "V 1/3/2023" (https://agr.mt.gov/_docs/industrialhemp-docs/MT-State-Hemp-Plan.pdf, 43 pages, opened in full 1 Nov 2026), and MDA's statement that Montana "is operating its 2026 State Hemp Program as a USDA approved Plan, effective January 27, 2023" (https://agr.mt.gov/hemp, accessed 1 Nov 2026).

Assembly instruction: state all three, labelled as different events. Do not write "USDA approved Montana's plan on 27 Jan 2023" as if that were the first approval, and do not write "approved 2020" as if the 2020 document were the current plan. The relationship between the 27 Jan 2023 effective date and the 3 Jan 2023 document (presumably the version that took effect on 27 Jan 2023) is the natural reading, but the approval letter itself was not opened — flag that link as Verify in §11 if the parent wants a single approval-date sentence.

1.5 ARM Title 4, Chapter 19 — range, and two cautions

MDA cites ARM 4.19.101–202 throughout. The rules actually opened or excerpted in this round are:

  • ARM 4.19.104, Program Fees (Cornell mirror, https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/montana/ARM-4-19-104, accessed 1 Nov 2026; NEW 2017 MAR p. 283, eff. 3/11/2017; AMD 2019 MAR p. 532, eff. 5/11/2019; AMD 2021 MAR p. 59, eff. 1/16/2021; AUTH: 80-1-102, 80-18-107, MCA; IMP: 80-18-102, 80-18-103, 80-18-106, 80-18-110, MCA). Full text in the Cornell version opened is in §10 below. Caution 1: that Cornell text states a flat $1,100 Montana State Hemp License fee and does not show the $850 Category A/B-only tier that MDA's 2026 forms and pages apply. See §10 for the conflict and the assembly instruction (use MDA's 2026 fees for 2026, flag the ARM text check).
  • ARM 4.19.201, Hemp Processing License and Fee — REPEALED. A 2019 MDA booklet (https://prod-agr.mt.gov/_docs/industrialhemp-docs/Hemp-MCA-ARM-Booklet.pdf, revised 9/3/2019, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026) prints ARM 4.19.201 (processing licence $2,500 floral/root extracts; $1,000 other processors) and ARM 4.19.202 (Hemp Processing for a Commodity Dealer). An Esper staging mirror excerpt (search result only, not an opened official page — do not upgrade it) states ARM 4.19.201 was repealed, NEW 2019 MAR p. 1369 eff. 8/24/19, REP 2022 MAR p. 1657 eff. 8/6/22. Caution 2: MDA still operates a Part A / Part B processor licence with the same $2,500 / $1,000 fees on its current processors page (opened 1 Nov 2026, §3 below), so the licence is live even if the old rule number is repealed. The current ARM citation for processor licensing must be confirmed on rules.mt.gov before final HTML — flag in §11. Do not cite ARM 4.19.201 as current without that check.
  • The State Hemp Plan, Appendix A, states sampling is completed "under authority of the Administrative Rules of Montana 4.19.110". That rule's text was not opened in this round — Verify on rules.mt.gov.
  • The full ARM 4.19.101–202 text was not opened rule-by-rule in this round; the Plan and MDA pages are the primary operational sources for §§2–5 below, with ARM 4.19.104 as the one fee rule opened in full. That limit is recorded in §11.

---

2. Grower licensing in full

2.1 What the licence authorises, and its term

A hemp licence "provides authorization for the production of hemp at a particular growing area by a particular individual or entity" (MDA hemp page, https://agr.mt.gov/hemp, accessed 1 Nov 2026). Under §80-18-102 (above), an individual who meets §80-18-103 may "plant, grow, harvest, possess, process, sell, or buy hemp, including cultivatable seeds or plants". The State Hemp Plan (§3, p. 5) adds that the Production Licence (Stage 2, below) "allows for the production, harvest, transportation, and sale of compliant hemp, including the processing of compliant hemp grown under the license", and that it "lists the licensed entity and each registered variety and strain with its corresponding growing location".

Term and form (State Hemp Plan §3, pp. 5–6, opened 1 Nov 2026):

  • "The hemp license application period is January through May 30. Licenses are issued on an annual basis and are valid until December 31 of the issuance year."
  • Licence number format: "the ANSI/FIPS State Code, followed by a 4-digit sequential license number and the year of issuance (e.g., 30-0001-22)".
  • "The application must be legible, complete, and include all required documentation and fees to obtain a license. To avoid a late fee, the application must be postmarked prior to the deadline of May 30."
  • "Licenses cannot be assigned or transferred." Transferability: NO — non-transferable. Do not describe a hemp licence as transferable.
  • "Any growing plant that is not harvested during the license period in which it was planted must be destroyed unless the license has been renewed."
  • "Any volunteer hemp plants growing in subsequent years must be destroyed or a licensee may register that grow location and cultivate and harvest the volunteer plants if they obtain permission from the department and pay fees associated with registering the grow location. The licensee must also update the location information with the USDA Farm Service Agency (FSA)."

2026 application deadlines (2026 Stage 1 form, http://agr.mt.gov/_docs/industrialhemp-docs/MT-Hemp-License-Application-Stage1.pdf, form version "V 1/14/26", accessed via search/open 1 Nov 2026; and MDA licensing page, http://agr.mt.gov/Topics/H-M/Hemp-Pages/Hemp-Licensing, opened 1 Nov 2026):

  • 2026 Indoor/Outdoor Licence "is valid from the time of issuance until December 31, 2026".
  • Outdoor application deadline: 30 May 2026. Indoor: no deadline (2026 form, p. 1).
  • Late outdoor applications are subject to a $100 late fee (MDA licensing page; see §10 for the three distinct $100 charges and the Verify item on whether the late-application $100 and the late/incomplete-documentation $100 are the same charge in practice).
  • "There will be no online submission option in 2026, forms must be mailed or emailed" (MDA licensing page). No hemp can be planted until the applicant has received a Planting Permit (same page, bold in original).

Renewal mechanics — Verify. The sources opened describe annual issuance to 31 December and destruction of unharvested plants "unless the license has been renewed", but no separate renewal form or renewal-only procedure was opened in this round. Do not describe renewal as a distinct lighter procedure; the safe assembly wording is "annual licence, valid to 31 December of the issuance year; renewal procedure beyond reapplication was not confirmed in the sources opened — Verify against MDA's 2026 renewal instructions".

2.2 The two progressive stages

MDA's licensing page (opened 1 Nov 2026) states:

"The department issues hemp licenses in (2) two progressive stages. Upon approval of the initial application, eligible applicants will be issued a Planting Permit so they may purchase seed or live plants, and seed or transplant hemp. To receive full licensure, the applicant must submit a Planting Report confirming information about the crop(s) that was planted. Successful candidates will be issued the second stage Production license. The Production license allows for the production and harvest of compliant hemp."

The Plan (§3, pp. 5–6) states the same, with the consequences spelled out:

"Any applicant that does not meet the conditions of the Production License will forfeit their Planting Permit and the crop will require destruction. Harvesting hemp without a state issued Production license is a violation of state and federal law."

Stage 1 — initial application. The Plan (§3, p. 6) requires:

  1. The applicant's full name, physical and mailing address, phone numbers, and email address. "If the applicant is a business, the Business License Number on file with the Montana Secretary of State's (SOS) office must be provided as well as an EIN number. A Business Name is only required if licensing as a registered business."
  2. "The full name, title and contact information of each person defined as a Key Participant."
  3. "For new applicants a copy of an FBI criminal history report for the applicant and each key participant associated with the operation" (see §2.4 on eligibility).
  4. For each variety or strain requested to be permitted: the name of the variety grown from certified seed or the common name of the strain; the quantity of land area requested to be permitted; the grow type (indoor or outdoor); the name and license number of the seed or live plant provider; and "a seed label and/or a COA verifying the name and THC levels of the hemp". Seed-provider and nursery rules are in §4 below.
  5. "Payment in full of the nonrefundable application fee" (fees in §10).
  6. "Signature of the applicant acknowledging the requirements, conditions, risks, and permissions associated with growing hemp."

MDA's Stage 1 page (https://agr.mt.gov/Topics/H-M/Hemp-Pages/Licensing-Stage1, opened 1 Nov 2026) adds: the application "cannot be processed and is not considered complete until all required documentation is received", and requires signatures acknowledging the Attestation Statement and the Risk Acknowledgment Statement. The 2026 Stage 1 form's Risk Acknowledgment (search excerpt, 1 Nov 2026) includes, in substance: Category C plants may be considered marijuana under state, local, or federal jurisdictions within and outside Montana; a Category C licensee who violates any part of the state plan with a culpable mental state greater than negligence shall be reported to USDA and federal and state law enforcement; law-enforcement actions related to Category C integrity are the sole responsibility of the grower, supplier, and landowner, with no liability attaching to MDA or its officers/representatives for Category C strains; remediation options for CBD strains testing greater than 0.3% Total THC no longer include chemical or physical extraction and/or dilution of THC; varieties/strains planted and developed in the United States prior to 2016 may not be compliant with the 2018 Farm Bill or federal hemp regulations; THC is tested in Montana using the federally required post-decarboxylation method, meaning the component measured is Total THC, which includes THCa; and the purchase/planting of non-certified or non-labeled seed carries inherent risk to seed quality, genetics, and potential elevated THC.

Stage 2 — Planting Report and Production Licence. The Plan (§3, pp. 6–7) requires a Planting Report "within 4 weeks of each growing event (planting seeds, receiving starter-plants, or rooting of clones)" listing, for each variety or strain: (1) FSA Lot ID (farm-tract-field-subfield); (2) the name of the variety grown from certified seed or the common name of the strain; (3) the quantity and units of land area planted (acres outdoors, or number of plants indoors); (4) the grow type (indoor or outdoor); (5) estimated harvest period; (6) primary market; (7) latitude and longitude of the growing area (legal description of the land); (8) physical county. The FSA Lot Number step is in §5.1 below: the producer reports growing locations and acreage to FSA, FSA assigns Lot Numbers, and those numbers "must be submitted to the Montana Department of Agriculture on the Stage 2 Hemp Planting Report" (MDA Stage 1 page; MDA FSA Acreage Reporting page, https://agr.mt.gov/Topics/H-M/Hemp-Pages/FSA-Acreage-Reporting, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026).

A 2021 Stage 2 form excerpt (secondary, older form — do not treat as the 2026 form) stated the Planting Report "must be received and approved before the Production License can be issued" and to "allow approximately 3–4 weeks for processing of the Production License". The 3–4-week processing figure is not confirmed for 2026 in the sources opened — mark Verify in §11 if the parent wants a processing-time sentence.

2.3 Cultivar categories A, B, C, D — the risk system that drives fees and sampling

State Hemp Plan §2 (pp. 3–4, opened 1 Nov 2026), definitions 19–22:

  • Category A: "varieties formally approved by the department, produced from 'certified' seed, and previously grown in Montana with a compliancy rating of 80% with a minimum of 5 official samples testing not greater than the Acceptable Hemp THC Level."
  • Category B: "varieties produced from 'certified' seed, not previously grown in Montana, and approved by other states or countries at a level equivalent to the department's standard."
  • Category C: "all other hemp varieties that an applicant reasonably believes will not produce a plant over the Acceptable Hemp THC Level at any time prior to harvest and are not in Category D."
  • Category D: "includes all varieties forbidden by any federal law or regulation or added by rule by the Department because of its inability to consistently produce hemp."

MDA's Stage 1 page (opened 1 Nov 2026): "The department has developed four risk-based categories of hemp cultivars. The cultivar you choose will determine the level of review your application receives and the level of testing for your crop. All cultivars that meet the definitions of Category A, B or C are allowed." Category D varieties may not be grown (Plan §5.1 context: "not grow Category D varieties" appears in the 2019 ARM booklet excerpt for ARM 4.19.103; the Plan's category system itself excludes D from "allowed" — state it that way, with the ARM 4.19.103 text as Verify in §11 since the current rule text was not opened).

Upgrade/downgrade (Plan §5.1, p. 9): "For a variety or strain to be upgraded, the department must receive a request from an interested party, and it must have a compliancy rating of 80% with a minimum of 5 official samples testing not greater than the Acceptable Hemp THC Level. A variety or strain may be removed or downgraded from the Category list if found to consistently produce hemp greater than 0.3% Total THC. Before any variety or strain is permitted to be grown in Montana, a review of its seed labeling (certification) and COA is completed to determine its Category."

"Certified" (Plan §2, definition 3) means certification from AOSCA, OECD, or another department-recognised official certifying agency that the variety is true to its genetic traits and testing has been completed to determine compliance regarding THC levels.

2.4 Eligibility: key participants and the 10-year felony bar

Key Participant (Plan §2, definition 24): "a person or persons who have a direct or indirect financial interest in the entity producing hemp, such as an owner or partner in a partnership. A key participant also includes a person in a corporate entity at executive levels including the chief executive officer, chief operating officer, and chief financial officer. This does not include other management positions like farm, field, or shift managers."

Criminal history. Plan §3 (p. 6): "Hemp program participation requires no prior convictions of felony charges related to a controlled substance in the previous 10 years. Verification of criminal history is required every 3 years." MDA's Stage 1 page (opened 1 Nov 2026) operationalises this under the 2018 Farm Bill and the USDA Final Rule, 7 CFR Part 990 (effective 22 Mar 2021): the department must review a criminal history report for new applicants — "you are considered a new applicant if you were not licensed in 2022" (that 2022 cut-off is MDA's page wording as opened; whether MDA still uses a 2022 cut-off for 2026 new applicants is Verify in §11) — via an FBI Identity History Summary (IdHS) for all individual applicants and, for a business entity, for each key participant. "IdHS reports must be dated within 60 days of the application and mailed to the department directly from the FBI."

Plan §10 adds the federal overlay: "A person with a state or federal felony conviction relating to a controlled substance is subject to a 10-year ineligibility restriction on producing hemp under the Agricultural Marketing Act of 1946. An exception applies to a person who was lawfully growing hemp under the 2014 Farm Bill before December 20, 2018, and whose conviction also occurred before that date." And: "Any persons who materially falsify any information in their application shall be ineligible to participate in the program."

No minimum age, residency, or Montana-entity requirement for hemp growers was located in the MCA, Plan, or MDA pages opened in this round. Do not state a hemp-grower age or residency rule — mark Verify in §11. (The 21+ age and 1-year residency rules in Run 1 belong to the marijuana programme, MCA §16-12-203, not to hemp.)

2.5 Research licence (in addition to the grower licence)

ARM 4.19.104(4) (Cornell, opened 1 Nov 2026): "The fee to license a research program under an existing license is $500 annually." State Hemp Plan §11 (pp. 19–20, opened 1 Nov 2026):

  • MDA "will issue a separate license designated as 'hemp research' to research facilities, whether the research is conducted by university research institutions, persons growing hemp under the direction of a university research institution or by private licensed entities conducting research not under the direction of a university research institution. This license will be in addition to the standard 'grower' license requirement for growing hemp."
  • "'Research' means growing hemp in a manner which the resulting hemp may not conform to the USDA guidelines in some manner. This includes but is not limited to the use of a pesticide not approved for hemp, efforts to lower a cultivar's THC level through hybridization, or development of new cultivars which are not from certified seed. Research must be conducted with the intent of improving or expanding upon the genetics and/or cultivation practices of hemp."
  • "Application for a research license is not open to the general public and requires a request by the applicant for a department review of the research project(s) prior to the department making an application available."
  • Conditions include: the licensed individual is the principal investigator; the application must include disposal procedures for all hemp, compliant and non-compliant, when research is complete; the entity may be subject to routine regulatory sampling including sampling/testing fees, unless it adopts an alternative sampling method that can ensure, at 95% confidence, that the cannabis to be subject to the method will not test above the Acceptable Hemp THC Level; record-keeping for not less than 3 years plus annual reporting; all research findings must be reported to the department; random inspections at a rate of 25% of all research entities licensed annually; proven/established varieties must be approved before entering a non-research phase and entering commerce; entities handling "hot" hemp must follow Controlled Substances Act requirements for handling marijuana; research hemp plants/parts cannot enter commerce, cannot be used for personal use or gain, can only be grown indoors unless the department grants an exception, can only be grown to establish improved genetics or other legitimate purposes approved by the department, must be tracked/reported from application through the licensing year, and shall be destroyed other than seed for propagation used for further research and potential future genetics.
  • University research institution exemptions: licence fees; key participants not required; research project goals may be generalised as long as plant numbers are inventoried; hemp can be grown outdoors without department permission; hemp can be grown with a goal other than establishing improved hemp genetics without department approval; registration of number of plants can be recorded in acreage for outdoor grows; landowner signatures not required.
  • Separately, §80-18-103(3) exempts "employees of the agricultural experiment station or the Montana state university-Bozeman extension service involved in research and extension-related activities" from the licensing requirements of Part 1. That statutory exemption and the Plan's research-licence regime are different mechanisms — do not merge them.

---

3. Processor licensing in full

3.1 Part A vs Part B

MDA's Hemp Processors page (https://agr.mt.gov/HempProcessors, opened 1 Nov 2026):

"The rules allow persons wanting to process hemp plants or plant parts to apply for a Hemp Processor license. The processor license allows licensees to produce derivatives that may be included in products for food, fiber, oils, supplements or drugs (excluding THC) for the wholesale or ingredient market."

"An applicant may apply for a license to process hemp floral and root extracts (Part A) and/or to process hemp grain, seed, seed oil and fiber (Part B) on the same application form. Part A is required of persons who process hemp floral or root extracts and the fee is $2,500 per calendar year. Part A also allows for the processing of hemp grain, seed, seed oil and fiber. Part B is voluntary and only allows for the processing of hemp grain, seed, seed oil and fiber, the fee is $1,000 per calendar year."

In table form:

PartCoversRequired or voluntaryFee (per calendar year, per physical location — see §3.2)
Part AHemp floral and root extracts; also allows grain, seed, seed oil, and fiberRequired for persons who process hemp floral or root extracts$2,500
Part BGrain, seed, seed oil, and fiber onlyVoluntary$1,000

The processor application form copy retrieved in Run 1 and re-located in this round (https://prod-agr.mt.gov/_docs/industrialhemp-docs/Hemp-Processor-License-Application.pdf, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026) states the same Part A / Part B structure and adds, for Part B: "The license to process grain, seed, seed oil and fiber is voluntary and offered only for verification of licensure." Form-version flag: that copy is headed "For year ending December 31, 2025" and "Eff 12/16/2024". No 2026 processor form was located in the searches opened in this round. The MDA processors page itself (opened 1 Nov 2026, undated) still states the same $2,500 / $1,000 fees, 31 December expiry, and year-round application period, so the fees and structure are carried as current on the strength of the live MDA page, but the 2026 form itself is Verify in §11 — do not cite the 2025 form as the 2026 form.

The 2019 MDA booklet's ARM 4.19.201 text (see §1.5, Caution 2) matches the same fees: "(1) A Montana Hemp Processing License for hemp floral and root extracts is required. (a) The fee for processing hemp floral and root extracts is $2,500 per year. (b) All other hemp processors of hemp grain, seed, seed oil, and fiber may apply for a Hemp Processing License for $1,000 per year." Because that rule number is shown as repealed in 2022 in a staging-mirror excerpt, the current ARM citation for processor licensing is Verify (see §11); the licence itself, on MDA's live page, is not in doubt.

3.2 Per-location rule, 1-mile radius, expiry, own-production exemption

MDA Hemp Processors page (opened 1 Nov 2026):

  • "The license expires on December 31 of each calendar year. The application period will remain open throughout the year."
  • "Persons who have a current and compliant hemp Grower's license can process their own hemp production without a Processor license." Own-production exemption: confirmed on the live MDA page. Note the qualifiers: "current and compliant" grower's licence, and "their own hemp production" — processing another grower's hemp is not covered by this sentence.
  • "Each physical location that conducts processing must be licensed separately and applied for on a separate form. A physical location is defined as processing and storage/warehouse structures within a 1-mile radius."
  • "A hemp processor is subject to an inspection that may include THC testing, pesticide testing or any other testing requested by law enforcement or the department. All hemp and the products/by-products derived from hemp must comply with state and federal law. THC extracted from hemp plants or hemp floral extraction must be legally disposed of in a manner consistent with state and federal regulations."
  • "Manufacturers purchasing from licensed hemp processors in Montana are not required to possess a hemp Processor's license." (Distinguish: a manufacturer purchasing from a licensed processor needs no processor licence; that sentence does not exempt a person who processes hemp plants/parts.)
  • "Hemp processors must comply with city, county, and tribal ordinances and laws. The approval of manufactured hemp derived products at the retail level continue to be subject to the laws and regulations of the United States Food and Drug Association [sic — FDA] and the Montana Department of Public Health and Human Services (DPHHS)."

The processor application form (2025 copy, above) also requires, where the applicant purchases hemp from Montana farmers (including through bailments), a copy of the applicant's contract form(s) for the application to be complete, with an initialled acknowledgment of §80-18-106 (the seed-type/contract filing section, §1.3 above). The form's attestation requires compliance with §§80-18-101 through 80-18-111, MCA, ARM 4.19.101–202, and "other state and federal regulations of DPHHS and FDA related to hemp processing", including consent to entrance of the property by department personnel for inspection and testing of purchased and processed materials.

Commodity Dealer point — Verify, do not state as current without a check. The 2019 booklet's ARM 4.19.202 is headed "Hemp Processing for a Commodity Dealer" and its excerpt states a Hemp Processing License is required to be exempt from the Commodity Dealer License requirement. A secondary business-plan summary (hempcbdbusinessplans.com, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026) repeats a Commodity Dealer Licence point for processors purchasing from growers. The current ARM 4.19.202 text was not opened in this round, so the commodity-dealer exemption is recorded as Verify in §11, not as a confirmed current rule.

No processor holder list was located. MDA's processors page as opened contains no holder list, count, or download. The Run 1 negative finding (no public MDA hemp grower/processor holder list found) is therefore not overturned for processors by anything opened in this round — but see the live-plant nuance in §4.3, and §11 for the precise wording the parent should use (a checked negative on the pages searched, not a proven claim that MDA holds no list anywhere).

---

4. Seed provider rules and the seed-provider list

4.1 Who may supply seed and live plants

State Hemp Plan §3 (p. 6, opened 1 Nov 2026), Stage 1 requirements: "Seed providers must be licensed in Montana. Nurseries must be licensed in their resident state. Producers who purchase live plants must submit a copy of the provider's resident state hemp license and verification of variety/strain (nursery label, seed label or Certification of Analysis (COA)) and be aware of live plant importation requirements."

MDA's Stage 1 page (opened 1 Nov 2026) and the 2026 Stage 1 form instructions (search excerpt, 1 Nov 2026) state the same rule in operational form: "The person providing seed and/or live plants to a Montana grower must be licensed to distribute into Montana — seed providers must be licensed in Montana; nurseries must be licensed in their resident state. Growers who purchase live plants must submit a copy of the provider's resident state hemp license and verification of variety/strain (nursery label, seed label or COA) and be aware of live plant importation requirements." MDA points growers to its Seed Program (https://agr.mt.gov/Seed-Program) and Nurseries pages for those requirements.

4.2 Seed Act licensing

MDA's Stage 1 page (opened 1 Nov 2026): "Any company selling agricultural seed including hemp seed in Montana must have the required seed license(s) outlined in Section 80-5-101 through Section 80-5-144, Montana Code Annotated (MCA)." MDA's Questions page (https://agr.mt.gov/Topics/H-M/Hemp-Pages/Hemp-Questions, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026) adds: "all entities that sell agricultural seed in Montana are required to follow the requirements of the Montana Seed Act. At a minimum, hemp seed sellers must have a Montana Seed Labeler license." The Questions page also states: "Seed can be purchased from any seed company. However, the department recommends that buyers do their research to ensure they are purchasing high quality and compliant varieties." and "Licensees can plant any seed reasonably believed to produce hemp. Seed varieties not previously grown in Montana or ASOCA/OECD certified are considered category C varieties and are grown at the licensee's own risk. All category C varieties are required to be tested for Total THC three weeks prior to harvest." (The "ASOCA" spelling is as it appears in the Questions page excerpt; the certifying body is AOSCA.)

4.3 The seed-provider list — what it is and is not

MDA's Stage 1 page (opened 1 Nov 2026):

"Current list of hemp seed providers licensed to distribute into Montana. This list is subject to change and will be updated periodically. The department is providing this list as a service to Montana hemp license applicants. It is not an endorsement, recommendation or guarantee of the company's seed in regard to germination, purity, or THC levels."

What it is: a list of seed providers licensed to distribute into Montana (seed dealers/labelers under the Seed Act), provided as a service to applicants, explicitly not an endorsement or guarantee of germination, purity, or THC levels.

What it is NOT:

  • It is not a grower register. It does not list Montana hemp growers, their acreage, or their licence status.
  • It is not a processor list. It does not list hemp processors.
  • It is not current by virtue of any copy retrieved in this round. The only seed-dealer list document retrieved in this round is "Montana Licensed (Hemp) Seed Dealers" (https://prod-agr.mt.gov/_docs/industrialhemp-docs/MT-Licensed-Seed-Dealers1.pdf, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026), headed "Updated 1/10/2022", with business name, city, state, zip, seed licence number, and seed licence type (e.g. Non-Resident Seed Dealer, Non-Resident Seed Labeler, Montana Seed Dealer, Montana Seed Labeler, Seed Conditioning). That 2022 document is stale and must not be quoted as the current list. The current list itself (the live linked list from the Stage 1 page) was not extracted name-by-name in this round — its contents are Verify in §11 if the parent wants provider names in the final file. Do not copy 2022 provider names into a 2026 file.

Live-plant nuance — do not over-read it into a grower list. State Hemp Plan §9 (p. 13, opened 1 Nov 2026), on sale of live hemp plants, states the seller "will need to review the hemp license list on the department's website and record the buyer's license number on the invoice. A sale may not be made to an individual not listed on the department's licensee list". That Plan sentence implies MDA maintains a licensee list that sellers can review for live-plant sales. No public grower/processor holder list URL was captured in the MDA pages opened in this round (hemp, licensing, Stage 1, processors, questions, inspections/sampling, disposal/remediation), and the Run 1 checked negative is not overturned. The correct assembly wording is therefore narrow: no public MDA hemp grower/processor holder list was located in the MDA pages opened in this round, notwithstanding Plan §9's reference to a department licensee list for live-plant sellers — whether that list is public, seller-gated, or on a page not opened is Verify (see §11). Do not state "MDA publishes no list" as a proven blanket negative, and do not state "MDA publishes a grower list" on the strength of Plan §9 alone.

---

5. Cultivation, reporting, sampling, testing, remediation, enforcement

All State Hemp Plan citations in this section are to the document dated 3 January 2023, version "V 1/3/2023" (https://agr.mt.gov/_docs/industrialhemp-docs/MT-State-Hemp-Plan.pdf, opened in full 1 Nov 2026), unless a different MDA page is named. The Plan is a 2023 document; where a 2026 MDA page differs, the 2026 page is noted. Verify whether a newer Plan version exists before final HTML (see §11).

5.1 Records and harvest reporting

Records. Plan §4 (pp. 7–8): "Each producer must keep all records related to planting, growing, harvesting, storage, destruction, distribution, sale and/or processing of hemp. The records must be made available for inspection for a minimum of 3 years." Producers "must report any updates and/or changes related to the licensed entity and hemp plantings, including contact information, variety, strain, location, and harvest dates to the Department as they occur."

FSA reporting. Each licence holder must report to the USDA Farm Service Agency (FSA): their hemp licence/authorization identifier in a USDA-prescribed format; hemp growing location(s), including street address to the extent possible and geospatial location; and acreage (or indoor square footage) of hemp planted, harvested, and, if applicable, disposed of or remediated. Producers must maintain a record of FSA-assigned Lot Numbers and report them to the department. MDA's FSA Acreage Reporting page (https://agr.mt.gov/Topics/H-M/Hemp-Pages/FSA-Acreage-Reporting, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026) instructs licensees to report hemp acreage to FSA by the established FSA acreage-reporting deadline (historically 15 July for most crops, but the FSA deadline was not confirmed in the sources opened — Verify in §11 if a date is wanted) and to submit FSA-assigned Lot Numbers to MDA on the Stage 2 Planting Report (§2.2).

Pre-harvest notification. Plan §4: "Each licensee must contact the Department a minimum of 30 days prior to harvest to confirm actual harvest date and schedule a pre-harvest inspection. Crops harvested without notification, regardless of category, are subject to destruction. Crops harvested later than the time allowed after official sampling are also subject to destruction. Crops not harvested within the allowed timeframe after official samples are collected may result in additional sampling, with the license holder responsible for additional fees and a non-compliance."

Annual Harvest/Disposal Report. Plan §4: "Each licensee must annually submit an Annual Hemp Harvest/Disposal Report (Appendix F5) listing harvest and/or disposal of any hemp grown", including: FSA Assigned Lot Number; variety or strain; total planted (acres or plants); quantity not harvested; total harvested; quantity disposed for non-compliant THC; harvest date; yield (pounds per Lot); intended use (CBD, fiber, grain, seed, propagative material); and explanation of discrepancies (crop failure, poor germination, etc.).

MDA's reports to USDA. Plan §4: department maintains and provides to USDA all required licensee contact information, licence number, legal description of land, licence status, and changes on USDA Form AMS-23 on the first day of each month (per Subpart F, §990.70 as cited in the Plan); monthly report to USDA listing disposal/remediation information for cannabis testing above the Acceptable Hemp THC Level on USDA Form AMS-24 (licence number, Lot location including geospatial location or other land descriptor, test results, disposal/remediation method, completion date); annual submission no later than 15 December to USDA of total acreage planted, harvested, and disposed of on USDA Form AMS-25; and department-lab sample test results reported to USDA using USDA's HeMP online platform.

5.2 Inspections and sampling

Plan §5 (pp. 8–9) and Appendix A (pp. 29–31):

  • "All hemp producers are subject to a routine annual regulatory inspection to verify hemp is not produced in violation of the program. New producers and producers under a corrective action plan will always be inspected. Inspections verify proper licensure, registration of growing locations, record-keeping, and general program compliance. Random annual audits may verify compliance for harvest date as it relates to sampling, harvest inventory, and raw product entering commerce."
  • The licence holder or authorised representative must be present at the growing site during inspection "unless the Department is given a good reason for inability to be present". The landowner on record can be an authorised representative if granted by the licence holder. Authorisation for entrance to, inspection of property, and collection of hemp samples for testing is granted by the licensee's signature as part of licensing. If neither holder nor authorised agent can be present, prior communications occur to assure both licensee and inspector know the Lots, locations, and pertinent information.
  • "Inspectors will be provided complete and unrestricted access at the designated inspection time or during normal governmental work hours to all hemp and other cannabis plants, whether growing or harvested, and all land, buildings, and other structures used for cultivation, handling, and storage."
  • Each licensee must notify the department with confirmation of harvest date a minimum of 30 days prior to actual harvest so inspection can be scheduled for sampling. Producers may not collect samples from their own growing facilities.
  • "Non-compliant Lots will be quarantined, with the licensee investigated and subject to enforcement in the Plan."
  • "Harvested Lots must maintain identity, not be commingled, and be traceable until official results indicate the legality of the Lot."
  • "Hemp inspections and sample collections will be conducted by full-time Department staff (sampling agents) trained to the standard established by USDA and the Department, including the USDA online training class, with training required before conducting inspections/sampling and an annual update. Qualifications/updates maintained by the Department. If any allowance is made for third-party sampling agents, the Department will ensure through record-keeping that third-party agents are certified to the USDA standard, producers are not allowed to collect their own samples, and sampling agents are not employed by or related to licensed producers they sample."

Who samples — answer: MDA full-time staff sampling agents, not the licensee, not the lab, and not (in the Plan version opened) third parties for official samples. Producers are expressly prohibited from collecting their own samples.

Performance-based sampling plan (Plan §5.1, p. 9). "All Lots are subject to sampling to verify the Acceptable Hemp THC Level is not exceeded. The Department uses risk-based and random approaches to meet performance-based goals. The Department shall sample the appropriate number of plants to ensure, at a confidence level of 95%, that no more than 1% of plants in the Lot would exceed the Acceptable Hemp THC Level. Risk determinations are based on seed certification standards and compliancy of varieties previously grown in Montana."

Sampling priorities (Plan §5.1):

  • 100% of Category C strains planted.
  • 20% of Category B varieties planted.
  • 10% of Category A varieties planted annually.
  • Each variety that tested above the Acceptable Level with a compliancy rating less than 80% in the previous year.
  • Any new variety not previously grown in Montana.
  • "Complaints of non-compliance will be investigated regardless of category or past compliance."

MDA's Inspections & Sampling page (https://agr.mt.gov/Topics/H-M/Hemp-Pages/Inspections-Sampling, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026) confirms in operational form: "All Category C strains must be inspected and sampled. Only a percentage of Category A and B varieties require inspection, but the harvest date of all Lots must be confirmed; the inspector will determine which Category A and B Lots require sampling. Crops harvested without notification, regardless of Category, are subject to destruction."

Sampling window (Plan Appendix A). The department's standard pre-harvest sampling window for official samples is within 3 weeks (21 days) prior to actual harvest. The USDA Final Rule allows a 30-day window, but the department states the 3-week window "provides a more uniform process and more accurate testing". The department may allow, under special circumstances due to resource constraints and/or unforeseen weather/harvest complications, a 30-day post-sampling harvest window if requested by the licence holder prior to harvest. Fibre crops: harvest dates must be reported within 30 days prior to harvest (cutting), like any hemp crop; if a crop is confirmed solely fibre, it may be sampled within 3 weeks prior to baling per the Performance Based Sampling Plan; fibre crops failing verification (ineligibility or delayed verification request) must be sampled prior to harvest.

Not required to be sampled: hemp plants meeting the definitions of Hemp Microgreens, Hemp Greens, and Hemp Transplants; and Hemp Mother Plants as defined in the Plan, not harvested and not entering commerce. Microgreens (Plan §2, definition 27): "immature seedlings for human consumption, cut above the soil/substrate line, harvested prior to flowering and not more than 14 days after germination, typically 2–3 inches, not taller than 5 inches, considered to have no THC". Quote that definition as the Plan's definition, not as a laboratory finding. Greens: leaves from immature plants germinated from seed, plants no more than 10 inches tall and not flowering.

Lot aggregation and post-harvest samples. Different Lots of the same variety/strain and same licence holder may be represented by a single sample as long as the variety/strain has similar characteristics, growing conditions, and harvest window. Post-harvest samples may be taken at department discretion but are for informational purposes only and are not recognised as official regulatory samples. Producers are notified of official results via correspondence including the official lab report and options for the protocol moving forward; the department's goal is to notify within 7 business days of sampling (Plan Appendix A) — state as a goal, not a guarantee. Test results determine eligibility to transport or process harvested materials.

Samples are department property. "All samples are property of the Department and will be properly disposed of by the State's testing laboratory according to laboratory procedures" (Plan §5 / Appendix A; MDA Inspections page repeats this).

Prior-years compliance data in the 2023 Plan — do NOT present as a 2026 rate. Plan §5.1 prints: total non-compliance 22.1%; Category A 3.5%; Category B 6.2%; Category C 27.1% (prior-three-years data in the Plan). The Plan's Lot-size/end-use table is for 2022 Lots. Neither figure is a 2026 rate, and no 2026 compliance rate was located in this round.

5.3 Testing — method, Total THC formula, measurement of uncertainty, DEA lab

Plan §6 (p. 11):

"The Montana State Hemp Program does not yet recognize or utilize third party testing of official hemp samples. The Department administers the Montana State University Analytical Lab located in Bozeman, MT and utilizes it for official state hemp sample testing."

Verify whether that "does not yet" statement still holds in 2026 — it is a 2023 Plan statement, and no 2026 MDA statement on third-party official testing was opened in this round. The baseline given for this task (USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing delayed to 31 Dec 2026) does not change the Montana official-sample route as described in the Plan, because Montana's official lab is already described as DEA-registered (below) — but that reasoning is Plan-version-dependent, so flag it in §11.

Lab requirements: minimum composite sample size 40 grams (for retention in case of referee analysis), maximum 120 grams (storage/destruction limits).

Testing method — this resolves the Run 1 Verify item for official testing:

"The Montana State Hemp Program uses a post-decarboxylation testing methodology using liquid chromatography which includes the calculation of THCA for a measurement of Total Delta9-THC on a dry weight basis."

Formula (Plan §6):

"% Total Delta9-THC = %Delta9-THC + (%Delta9-THCA x 0.8772)"

Plan §2, definition 41 ("Total THC") states the same concept with factor 0.877: "Total THC: the value determined after the process of decarboxylation, or the application of a conversion factor if the testing methodology does not include decarboxylation, that expresses the potential total delta-9 tetrahydrocannabinol (THC) content derived from the sum of the THC and THCA content and reported on a dry weight basis. This post-decarboxylation value of THC can be calculated by using a chromatograph technique using heat, such as gas chromatography, through which THCA is converted from its acid form to its neutral form, THC. Thus, this test calculates the total potential THC in a given sample. The total THC can also be calculated by using a liquid chromatograph technique, which keeps the THCA intact. This technique requires the use of the following conversion: [Total THC = (0.877 x THCA) + THC — AUDIT: use Total THC = %Delta-9-THC + (%Delta-9-THCA × 0.8772) (State Hemp Plan §6); Plan §2’s 0.877 is the §6 factor rounded, not a separate alternative formula.]"

Audit resolved: 0.8772 is the §6 formula figure; 0.877 is its rounding, not a separate formula. The 2026 Stage 1 Risk Acknowledgment confirms the method in plain language: "THC is tested in Montana using the federally required post-decarboxylation method, meaning the component measured to determine the compliance of hemp is Total THC, which includes THCa."

Delta-8 counts as delta-9 for this purpose: Plan §2, definition 41 ends: "Delta-8 will be considered for this purpose to be the same as Delta-9." That is a testing-method statement. It is distinct from the Schedule I listing and synthetic ban in §8; keep the two questions separate in assembly.

Acceptable Hemp THC Level (Plan §2, definition 1): "the application of the measurement of uncertainty to the reported total delta-9 tetrahydrocannabinol (THC) content concentration level on a dry weight basis producing a distribution or range that includes 0.3 percent or less."

Measurement of Uncertainty (MU) (Plan §6): the department uses its official MU of 0.0453% in addition to the standard 0.3% Total THC allowance. Plan examples, quoted exactly as Plan examples (do not generalise them into a different rounding rule):

  • Compliant: result 0.3503 ± MU 0.0453, range 0.305 to 0.3956 (0.305 rounds to 0.30).
  • Violation: result 0.3504 ± MU 0.0453, range 0.306 to 0.3957 (0.306 rounds to 0.31).

Lab credentials (Plan §6). "The laboratory is a DEA-registered lab (License Number: RS0591506) in regard to cannabis testing, is accredited to ISO/IEC 17025 for the specific tests listed on A2LA Certificate Number 2694.01 and is certified in forage analysis by NFTA. Formal chain of custody procedures are in place and documented for each official sample by sampling agents and laboratory staff to ensure plant materials of sampled Lots are not commingled." Verify the DEA licence and A2LA certificate numbers are still current before final HTML — they rest on the 2023 Plan.

5.4 Remediation and disposal

Plan §10.2 (pp. 16–18) and MDA's Disposal & Remediation page (https://agr.mt.gov/Topics/H-M/Hemp-Pages/Disposal-Remediation-Hemp, accessed via search 1 Nov 2026):

  • Any Lot testing above the Acceptable Hemp THC Level from an official sample is non-compliant and must be either properly disposed of or remediated according to department procedures. All plants/parts of a non-compliant Lot destined for remediation or disposal must be clearly labelled as such. The licensee must notify the department, on notification of an above-Acceptable result, of the decision to remediate or dispose.
  • Remediation eligibility: a Lot testing above the Acceptable Level and less than 1.0% Total THC may be considered for remediation; less than 2.0% Total THC if verified by department personnel to be marketed solely as a fibre commodity, from a pre-harvest sample. MDA's Disposal page states the same thresholds: "A Lot of hemp testing above the Acceptable Hemp THC Level and less than 1.0% Total THC may be considered for remediation. Crops verified by the department to be marketed solely as a fiber commodity may be considered for remediation if test results are less than 2.0% Total THC from a pre-harvest sample." Remediation must include all plants/parts of the Lot.
  • Remediation methods:
  1. Separating and destroying non-compliant flowers including buds, trichomes, "trim", and "kief", while retaining stalks, leaves, and grain or seed. Acceptable removal methods include removal by hand or mechanical equipment, or through retting for solely fibre crops, with proper disposal as outlined, maintaining separation of salvageable stalks, leaves, grain/seed. Seeds removed during remediation shall not be used for propagative purposes.
  2. Shredding the entire hemp plant including all flowers, buds, trichomes, "trim", "kief", leaves, stalks, and seed or grain to create a homogenous "biomass", via shredders, composters, or specialty mechanical equipment.
  • Chemical or physical extraction and/or dilution of THC is NO LONGER an allowed remediation process. MDA's Disposal page notice: "Chemical or physical extraction and/or dilution of THC is no longer an allowed process for remediation of non-compliant hemp lots." 2026 Stage 1 Risk Acknowledgment item 5 confirms: "Remediation options for Cannabis strains grown for CBD production and testing greater than 0.3% Total THC no longer include chemical or physical extraction and/or dilution of THC." Do not list extraction/dilution as a current remediation option.
  • Regardless of method, remediated material must be resampled and retested to ensure compliance before entering commerce. Material testing above the acceptable level after remediation is non-compliant and must be destroyed via a disposal option. Remediated material should be separated from compliant hemp, clearly labelled/demarcated as "hemp for remediation purposes", and stored/labelled/demarcated apart from other Lots and from compliant hemp nearby. It should not leave the labelled/demarcated area until a compliant test result is received or until it will be destroyed. The licensee is responsible for inspection, sampling, and testing fees related to resampling. Official re-samples are tested per Plan protocol. On a case-by-case basis, third-party re-sampling/re-testing may be allowed; the producer cannot collect their own samples.
  • Disposal required for: lack of records indicating hemp was produced by a properly licensed producer or on a registered growing site; lack of control by the licence holder; official test results showing Total THC exceeding 2.0% dry weight; official test results showing Total THC exceeding 0.3% dry weight without an approved remediation plan.
  • Disposal must include all plants/parts of the Lot. Disposal must be conducted either by using a DEA-registered reverse distributor or law enforcement, or on site at the farm or hemp production facility. Allowable methods: plowing, tilling, or disking plant material into the soil; mulching, composting, chopping, or bush mowing plant material into green manure; burning plant material; burying plant material into the earth and covering with soil.
  • For Lots/plants/parts required to be disposed of by the department, the producer must request approval through submission of a corrective action plan; on approval, perform disposal accordingly within the given timeframe; to complete the process, provide pictures or videos showing proof of destruction and the Witness Statement form signed by the licence holder and an independent third party verifying destruction. Plan internal inconsistency — flag in §11: the Plan table of contents lists "F7 — Witness Statement" (Appendix F, p. 41–43 area), while §10.2 text refers to "Appendix F9" for the Witness Statement. Do not resolve the appendix letter/number silently.
  • The licence holder is responsible for assuring non-compliant plants/parts are rendered into a non-retrievable or non-ingestible form and non-viable for further propagation, distribution, or sale, and for control/destruction of volunteer plants not registered under an active licence. Any disposal/destruction, voluntary or by department order, must comply with state and federal regulations.

5.5 Enforcement: non-compliance, negligent violations, culpable mental state greater than negligence

Plan §10 and §10.1 (pp. 14–16):

  • Any Lot testing above the Acceptable Level from an official sample is non-compliant and must be disposed of or remediated per department procedures. Each violation will be recorded against the licence holder and may be considered a separate offence. For any violation of state/federal hemp regulations, the department determines whether it will be treated as non-compliance, negligent, or culpable mental state greater than negligence, considering nature, outcome, and any financial/health harm, and weighing the standard of care a reasonably prudent person would have exercised in a similar situation when determining culpable mental state greater than negligence and assigning penalties. Such violations may be subject to other state, tribal, or federal regulatory action.
  • One strike per year cap (negligent): "Per federal regulations as cited in the Plan, a maximum of one strike per calendar year can be issued for one or more negligent violations to an entity violating state/federal hemp program regulations."
  • Felony bar (restated here once, not as a second bar): "A person with a state or federal felony conviction relating to a controlled substance is subject to a 10-year ineligibility restriction on producing hemp under the Agricultural Marketing Act of 1946. An exception applies to a person who was lawfully growing hemp under the 2014 Farm Bill before December 20, 2018, and whose conviction also occurred before that date." This is the same 10-year bar as §2.4; do not double-count it.

Non-Compliance. Hemp testing above the Acceptable Level and not greater than 1.0% Total THC, or the negligence threshold defined by USDA, whichever is greater, from an official sample. A Corrective Action Plan (CAP) must be provided by the licence holder and approved by the department. "A Non-Compliance not corrected will result in a Negligent violation. A Negligent violation may also be recorded for every five Non-Compliances issued." A Non-Compliance will not be subject to criminal enforcement action by federal, tribal, state, or local government.

Negligent violations include, but are not limited to: failure to obtain a hemp licence; failure to provide, or accurately provide, a legal description of land on which hemp is produced; failure to maintain required records; failure to submit complete and accurate information on documents pertaining to hemp planted, harvested, sampled, remediated, or under investigation; conducting harvest later than the time allowed post-sampling without prior approval; producing Cannabis with Total THC greater than 1.0%, or the USDA negligence threshold, whichever is greater, on a dry-weight basis. A CAP must be provided and approved.

Three-in-five-year bar — confirmed, this is a Montana Plan rule, not an imported rule: "Three negligent violations in a five-year period → ineligible to produce hemp for five years from the date of the third violation." A Negligent violation will not be subject to criminal enforcement action.

Only CAP for hemp testing greater than 2.0% Total THC: proper disposal of the entire Lot.

Culpable mental state greater than negligence. Plan §2 defines it as acting intentionally, knowingly, willfully, or recklessly. Such violations cannot be mitigated with a CAP. The department will notify the respondent, report the respondent to the US Attorney General, the Montana Attorney General, and the local law enforcement agency, and begin proceedings for suspension of licensure. Findings of a violation greater than negligent will result in revocation of licensure. Examples include, but are not limited to: three negligent violations in a five-year period; failure to disclose a felony conviction relating to a controlled substance under state/federal law within the past 10 years while possessing a state hemp licence; material falsification of information pertaining to hemp planted, harvested, sampled, remediated, or under investigation; intentionally, knowingly, or recklessly producing hemp greater than 1.0% Total THC dry weight.

CAP procedure (§10.1). The producer shall comply with a department-approved CAP to correct an eligible violation within 30 days of receipt of an approved plan. If correction cannot be completed within 30 days, the producer may request an extension of up to 2 years from the date of the violation. Completion must be reported in writing to the department or verified via inspection; extended CAPs include quarterly updates due the first day of each quarter from violation to completion. Written completion documentation must include completion date, status/form of final product, and signature of licensed individual or responsible person. The department will inspect to determine whether the CAP was implemented as submitted. If a subsequent violation occurs while a CAP is in place, a new CAP must be submitted with a heightened level of quality control, staff training, and quantifiable action measures. Any person whose violation is not corrected as outlined in the CAP may have their licence(s) suspended or terminated after 30 days' written notice. CAPs must be submitted in writing on a department-approved form within 15 business days of notification of the violation, including: licence holder's name and licence number; FSA-assigned Lot Number; Lot quantity planted, harvested, and resolution material; list of responsible parties; reasonable timeframe for implementation; all information necessary to prove compliance; planted Lot location, location of harvested material, location of resolution; variety/strain; equipment and method used. An administrative hearing or informal appeal under the Montana Administrative Procedure Act is available if a party timely disputes the violation level or CAP agreement. The department may reinstate the licence if good cause is shown and reinstatement fees are paid — the reinstatement fee amount was not stated in the Plan text opened; Verify, do not invent an amount.

All official sample test results and violations will be documented, with the respondent notified in formal correspondence by email, USPS mail, and/or USPS certified mail.

---

6. Hemp on tribal reservations

6.1 MDA has no authority within reservation boundaries — verbatim

MDA hemp page (https://agr.mt.gov/hemp, opened 1 Nov 2026):

"The state of Montana does not have authority over hemp grown within the boundaries of tribal reservations. That authority rests with the tribal nation on the reservation or the U.S. Department of Agriculture (USDA), depending on the status of the resident tribe's hemp plan. Any person interested in growing hemp within reservation boundaries, whether a tribal member or not, must contact their resident tribe or the USDA to request information on hemp licensing requirements."

State Hemp Plan §1 (p. 2, opened 1 Nov 2026) states the same limit: the Plan's authority "does not extend to hemp grown within the boundaries of the reservations located in Montana" and lists eight: Blackfeet Tribe of the Blackfeet Reservation; Chippewa Cree Tribe of the Rocky Boy's Reservation; Confederated Salish & Kootenai Tribes of the Flathead Reservation; Crow Tribe of the Crow Reservation; Fort Belknap Tribes of the Fort Belknap Reservation; Fort Peck Tribes of the Fort Peck Reservation; Little Shell Chippewa Tribe; Northern Cheyenne Tribe of the Northern Cheyenne Reservation.

The 2026 Stage 1 form instructions add: "For applicants intending to grow within tribal boundaries, please note updated licensing requirements in the Instructions." The updated tribal-boundary instructions themselves were not extracted in this round — Verify what they require before final HTML; do not summarise them from the form cover line alone. The form also directs tribal-nation contact information to https://tribalnations.mt.gov/tribalnations (as printed on the form; that link was not opened in this round).

6.2 What the full USDA AMS table shows — five Montana tribal entries

USDA AMS List of USDA-approved Hemp Plans (https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/state-and-tribal-plan-review, updated 17 Feb 2026, opened in full 1 Nov 2026). Montana state entry: "Montana | MT Approved Plan (pdf)*" (the asterisk meaning approved under the federal final rule effective 22 Mar 2021). Montana tribal entries on that table, exactly as listed:

Tribal entry (USDA table wording)USDA table status
Assiniboine and Sioux Tribes of the Fort Peck ReservationUSDA Hemp Producer License
Blackfeet Nation Tribal CouncilApproved Plan (pdf)
Chippewa CreeApproved Plan (pdf)
Confederated Salish & Kootenai Tribes of the Flathead ReservationUSDA Hemp Producer License
Fort Belknap Indian CommunityApproved Plan (pdf)

Correction to Run 1 / Parts 9–10: those tracks mentioned only Fort Peck and CSKT. The full table shows Blackfeet, Chippewa Cree, and Fort Belknap as Approved Plans as well. The full table opened in this round supersedes the two-entry summary.

Naming note: USDA lists "Chippewa Cree" without a reservation qualifier; the Plan lists "Chippewa Cree Tribe of the Rocky Boy's Reservation". Treat them as the same entity for assembly, but flag the naming variant in §11 rather than silently merging two different names if the parent wants strict name matching.

Descriptor note: Montana's state plan carries an asterisk ("Approved *", approved under the final rule eff. 22 Mar 2021). Blackfeet, Chippewa Cree, and Fort Belknap are listed as "Approved Plan (pdf)" without an asterisk in the table text opened. Do not over-interpret the asterisk difference; approval dates for those three tribal plans were not extracted from the table, and their plan PDFs were not opened. Verify plan dates if the parent wants them.

Distinction that must be preserved: "USDA Hemp Producer License" (Fort Peck, CSKT) is USDA direct licensing of producers, not a tribal plan. "Approved Plan (pdf)" (Blackfeet, Chippewa Cree, Fort Belknap) is a USDA-approved tribal hemp plan. Do not describe Fort Peck/CSKT as having an approved tribal plan, and do not describe Blackfeet/Chippewa Cree/Fort Belknap as USDA-direct-licensed producers, on this table alone.

6.3 Tribes not on the USDA table — Unavailable, not "no programme"

Crow Tribe, Northern Cheyenne Tribe, and Little Shell Tribe are not listed on the 17 Feb 2026 USDA table as opened. That is not a finding that they have no hemp programme: it means they do not appear on that table under those names. Their status is Unavailable/Verify — do not write "no programme". Because MDA states it has no authority within reservation boundaries, a state licence is not the fallback route for those reservations on the sources opened; the route is to check the resident tribe or USDA directly, per MDA's own instruction (§6.1).

6.4 Affirmative-defence wording question

§80-18-111 (quoted in §1) provides an affirmative defence where the defendant "was growing or processing hemp in compliance with this part or a tribal plan approved by the United States department of agriculture". On its face, that wording mentions a USDA-approved tribal plan; it does not expressly mention USDA producer licences (Fort Peck/CSKT). Do not extend or narrow that defence by interpretation in final HTML; quote it and flag the producer-licence wording question in §11.

6.5 Schedule III / "state-licensed" tribal question — Verify (given baseline)

Per the task's given federal baseline, split scheduling (medical to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026, 91 FR 22714) applies to state-licensed medical marijuana. Tribal hemp plans and USDA producer licences in this section are hemp authorisations, not state-licensed medical marijuana licences, and must not be conflated with that question. Whether any tribe operates a medical marijuana programme that counts as "state-licensed" for Schedule III purposes is Verify — no such tribal medical programme was located in this Part 7 round, and this section makes no claim about one.
