Tribal ordinances and codes: none located in the Part 9–10 searches (checked negative for the sources searched; a tribe-by-tribe enumeration was not done — V-22). Do not cite any tribal ordinance without a tribal-government source. The only tribal-government document located was the Fort Belknap HB 952 letter (§9.6) — a policy letter, not an ordinance.

State-tribal agreements and compacts: no cannabis-specific compact was located; HB 952 was vetoed, so there is no compact statute. Whether any cannabis agreement exists under other authority (State-Tribal Cooperative Agreements Act) is Verify, not established — check the Governor's Office of Indian Affairs and DOR agreement records before stating either that one exists or that none exists (V-22). Do not state "no agreement exists" as a proven negative.

Tribal hemp — corrected by Part 7: the full USDA AMS plan table (updated 17 February 2026, opened in full in Part 7) shows five Montana tribal entries — Fort Peck and CSKT on USDA hemp producer licences, plus Blackfeet, Chippewa Cree and Fort Belknap on approved tribal plans. This supersedes the two-entry statement in Run 1 and in the original Part 9–10 excerpt. Crow, Northern Cheyenne and Little Shell are not listed — marked Unavailable, not "no programme". See Part 7 §6 for the full table and the affirmative-defence wording question.

10.4 Tribal "state-licensed" Schedule III and 280E question — Verify, do not decide

Do not decide this question in this file. For treating a tribal programme as "state-licensed": a combined-use licensee is DOR-licensed under Chapter 12, pays state fees ($7,500 per year) and state taxes, and operates under state rules — arguably within the "state-licensed medical" wording; a DOJ order per secondary legal analysis (Vicente LLP) "specifically references 'FDA-approved drug products containing marijuana and marijuana subject to state-issued licenses'". Against, or uncertain: a tribal programme under tribal sovereignty or a tribal ordinance (not a DOR licence) may not be "state-licensed"; tribal programmes are "conspicuously absent from both the Order and the discussion" (Vicente; MMJ Daily); tribal businesses "unable to register" as DEA registrants "may be cut off from newly-registered state licensees" (MMJ Daily). Also: "state-licensed medical marijuana business" is not yet clearly defined, and whether DEA registration is required for 280E relief is disputed among tax professionals (Cannabis Business Times). Tribal governments and wholly owned tribal-law-chartered entities are already exempt from federal income tax (IRS guidance published 15 December 2025, per Vicente) — a different question from 280E, which does not answer "state-licensed". No Montana or federal guidance answering this question for Montana tribes was located. Do not state that tribal medical activity is, or is not, Schedule III or 280E-relieved. [AUDIT: in any event, any Schedule III / 280E treatment could apply only to the state-licensed medical portion of a combined-use licensee’s activity; the adult-use portion remains Schedule I and fully subject to §280E.]

10.5 Federal land

36 CFR §2.35(b) text (via the NPS Klondike Gold Rush – Seattle Unit laws page, nps.gov/klse, and a CFR mirror, law.lawstack.com/cfr/36/2.35/; eCFR not opened directly — V-24): (b)(1) delivery of a controlled substance is prohibited except distribution by a practitioner in accordance with applicable law; (b)(2) possession is prohibited "unless such substance was obtained by the possessor directly, or pursuant to a valid prescription or order, from a practitioner acting in the course of professional practice or otherwise allowed by Federal or State law"; (c) presence in a park area under the influence to a degree endangering the person, another person, or damaging property or park resources is prohibited. "Controlled substance" is defined at 36 CFR §1.4 by reference to CSA Schedules I–V (21 U.S.C. 812). Application: adult-use remains Schedule I federally, so Montana adult-use permission does not travel onto National Park Service land. Whether state-licensed medical marijuana falls within the "otherwise allowed by Federal or State law" exception after the 28 April 2026 Schedule III move is not answered by any NPS source located — Verify (V-24). Do not advise that medical marijuana is, or is not, possessable in a park.

Yellowstone enforcement context (secondary, dated, not a rule): November 2025 secondary articles report a 29 September 2025 Department of Justice reversal of Biden-era guidance on minor federal-land drug offences and a United States Attorney for Wyoming statement that federal-land marijuana laws "have been, and will continue to [be] enforce[d]" and "rigorously prosecuted", with simple possession described as a Class B misdemeanour (up to 6 months' jail and a $5,000 fine in those accounts); a JustAnswer thread describes a Yellowstone violation notice for about 1–1.5 grams coded 36 CFR 2.35 with about $400 collateral forfeiture. All secondary or anecdotal — do not present the $400 figure or "rigorous prosecution" as a current Montana-specific NPS rule without the DOJ memo and the District of Wyoming collateral schedule (V-24). Secondary attorney analysis for Yellowstone and Grand Teton (puzzlegroup.com) must not be quoted as NPS policy. Glacier National Park: no Glacier-specific NPS cannabis rule was located — Unavailable. Other national parks, forests and BLM land in Montana: no Montana-specific order was located — Unavailable (general federal principle only; do not state that Yellowstone and Glacier have identical local rules without their superintendents' compendia). Federal enclaves: none located — Unavailable. Hemp on federal land: no NPS, Forest Service or BLM hemp-specific Montana rule was located — Verify; do not assume hemp is freely possessable in parks.

10.6 Transport, interstate movement and borders

In-state production only: §16-12-203(3), verbatim (official MCA page): "Marijuana for use pursuant to this chapter must be cultivated and manufactured in Montana unless federal law otherwise allows for the interstate distribution of marijuana." Federal law does not currently allow interstate distribution, so in-state production only.

Transporter rules: §16-12-222 (Run 1 summary; full text not re-opened — V-25): transporter licence valid 2 years, transporter responsible after taking control, may contract with multiple licensees, may deliver only to licensed premises or cardholders, may not deliver to adult-use consumers, delivery person must hold a worker permit and be a current employee, seed-to-sale manifests required; own-product transport by cultivator, manufacturer, dispensary and lab licensees and their employees without a transporter licence (§16-12-222(4)). ARM 42.39.413 (Cornell and Esper mirrors; official ARM page not opened — V-25), key provisions: (1) a transporter may physically distribute and deliver to a licensed premises and to registered cardholders within Montana presenting a valid registry identification card; (2) proof of a valid Montana driver's licence required; (3) all distribution and delivery must (a) occur in a motor vehicle as defined by ARM 42.39.102, (b) depart from a licensed premises and be delivered to a licensed premises or a cardholder's address (the cardholder must provide a valid registry identification card before transport is completed), (c) be accompanied by a seed-to-sale transport manifest (departure and arrival addresses and licence or cardholder numbers, dates and estimated times, transporter driver's licence number, vehicle make, model and plate, names and signatures of accompanying licensee(s) or employee(s), complete product description including name and type, amount verified by count or weighing device under §30-12-203 and ARM 24.351.101, and RFID tag numbers), and (d) be accomplished within seven days from departure; (4) an overnight stay on the planned direct route must be identified on the manifest beforehand, the in-transit product accepted into the storage facility's inventory or another licensed premises, and stored in a licensee's licensed premises. A transporter or employee must not sell product or transport directly to consumers. The "must not leave Montana" ARM subsection number was not extracted — Verify (V-25); the condition appears in the City of Billings transporter attestation ("Shall not leave the state of Montana while any amount of marijuana or marijuana product is in the motor vehicle", billingsmt.gov) and in DOR's "instate only" framing. CCD intrastate guidance PDF (revenue.mt.gov/files/Cannabis/Intrastate-Transportation-of-Marijuana-by-Licensees.pdf; excerpt only, full vehicle conditions Verify — V-25): "Marijuana transportation (instate only) by licensed marijuana businesses is allowed in certain circumstances"; any person transporting must have a valid CCD worker permit; vehicles must be lockable (excerpt truncated); all transportation except marijuana legally possessed by a cardholder or adult-use consumer requires a valid Metrc manifest, with copies and delivery receipts presented to law enforcement or authorised Department employees on request per ARM 42.39.413(15); within 7 days; "Dispensaries may deliver to registered cardholders only"; "Delivery of adult-use marijuana is prohibited in the state of Montana"; transporter licences are only required for third-party transportation. Transporter counts (CannBus secondary): 9 (August 2024), 12 (August 2026); fee $10,000 for 2 years (CannBus — Verify against ARM 42.39.102, V-25).

Personal transport and vehicles: DOR "Know Before You Go – Personal Marijuana Possession on Montana's Roads" (revenue.mt.gov/card/cannabis/education/know-before-you-go; search excerpt, page not opened in full — V-26): §16-12-106 possession and transport up to 1 ounce (up to 8 grams concentrate, up to 800 mg THC in solid edibles); the $100 vehicle fine is now resolved to its statutory basis in Part 12 — §61-8-1027 (see Part 12 §12(c)); "Crossing state lines in a motor vehicle with personal, adult-use marijuana, marijuana products, or marijuana paraphernalia is prohibited under any circumstances. There is no exception for traveling across state lines with medicinal marijuana; it remains prohibited." Driving under the influence is resolved in Part 12: §61-8-1002 has a per se THC prong at 5 ng/mL or more, excluding inactive metabolites, plus an impairment prong and an under-21 zero-tolerance THC prong — see Part 12, which supersedes the Part 9–10 note that the official §61-8-1002 text had not been opened.

Interstate and through-transport: marijuana interstate transport is prohibited (§16-12-203(3) and the DOR no-exception statement). Through-transport of marijuana through Montana: no exception was located — mark Verify, do not invent one (V-27) (the sources point against an exception, but that inference is not a located rule). Hemp through-transport: no Montana-specific rule was located (V-27) — the federal 2018 Farm Bill framework was not re-researched per the baseline instruction; SB 375 and §16-12-117 are sale bans, not transit rules; do not state a Montana hemp through-transport right or prohibition without a direct Montana source; note the sale-versus-transit distinction. See also Part 7 §7 (no Montana through-transport or roadside-stop rule located).

Cross-border (official status only, no enforcement speculation): Idaho: marijuana illegal, no medical programme, no cannabis licences, Schedule I in Idaho (§37-2705), hemp retail zero-THC rule (Office of Drug Policy, February 2025) — per the Run 1 Idaho dossier (1 November 2026). Wyoming: marijuana illegal, no medical programme, no cannabis licences; 2024 SF0032 (SEA 24, effective 1 July 2024) banned synthetics, expanded THC to analogues and isomers, delta-8 Schedule I, upheld by the Tenth Circuit on 27 October 2025 — per the Run 1 Wyoming dossier. North Dakota, South Dakota and Canada (British Columbia, Alberta, Saskatchewan) were not researched in Part 9–10 — no status, border rule or reciprocity may be stated for them (V-28). Do not state that cannabis may be taken across the Canada–United States border in either direction.

Border counties: no official cross-border sales study was located in Run 1, and none was located in the Part 9–10 re-check — do not estimate cross-border sales; do not attribute out-of-state-buyer demand to any county (Dawson, Richland, Lincoln, Sanders, Ravalli, Beaverhead, Flathead, Glacier, Hill, Blaine, Valley, Sheridan and Roosevelt are named as examples not to attribute). Official context only: the I-190 county table (border Yes including Flathead, Glacier, Lincoln, Sanders, Roosevelt, Sheridan, Valley and Richland; border No including Beaverhead, Carter, Daniels, Dawson in 2020 and Phillips and Toole); the Dawson and Custer moves are §16-12-301 local-approval changes, not cross-border sales evidence.

Parts 11–12 research summary. All sources accessed 1 November 2026 unless a different date is stated in the source itself. ARM citations obtained via the Cornell LII mirror are identified as such; re-check every ARM citation against rules.mt.gov for final reliance, as Cornell states quarterly updates and several cross-references in its text are stale (37.107 numbering).

Report received 2026-11-01 (coordinator completion 17:17:53 UTC). All sources accessed 1 Nov 2026. Full handoff text is the authority; this is a condensed assembly aid. Do NOT present Run 2 as complete — Parts 7–8 still outstanding at time of filing.

PART 11: TESTING, SECURITY, RETAIL OPERATIONS, COMPLIANCE

11(a) Testing

  • Statutory duty MCA §16-12-209 (official leg.mt.gov page opened; hist. I-190 §17, Ch. 576 L. 2021, Ch. 712 L. 2023): cultivator/manufacturer/adult-use or medical dispensary may not sell until tested by a testing lab and meeting requirements; licensee pays. Sampling protocol by Dept rule incl. batch sizes. Dept rules must cover potency + contaminants. Lab visual inspection per batch (foreign matter, debris, insects, visible mold). Dept sets acceptable levels for moisture, pesticides, residual solvents, mold, mildew, foreign matter, debris, insects, other contaminants. Lab issues certificate of analysis + reports to seed-to-sale (§16-12-105). Retest on licensee request per Dept rules. Label must indicate tested. Dept collects/destroys failing samples, except THC-only excess may be disposed of by other means per rule; Dept may contract duties.
  • Labs MCA §16-12-206 (official page opened; I-190 §12, Ch. 576, Ch. 712): may measure THC, THCA, CBD, CBDA; test pesticides, solvents, moisture, mold, mildew, other contaminants; may transport samples. Scientific Director: doctorate in chemical/biological sciences + 2 yrs post-degree lab experience, OR master's + 4 yrs. All owners/employees fingerprint/background check per §16-12-129; lab may not be owned/operated/staffed by a felony convict. Qualify: staff proficiency, equipment/instrumentation per rule incl. certifying at required sensitivity, insurance/bonding per rule, capacity to serve rural areas, passed Dept-approved proficiency programme. Tests: pre-sale cultivator/manufacturer samples, Dept inspection samples, consumer/cardholder samples.
  • ARM 42.39.603 (Cornell mirror; NEW 2019, AMD 2022, TRANS from 37.107.301 2023, AMD 2024): applicant docs incl. insurance/bonding certs per ARM 42.39.417, Scientific Director résumé/transcripts/degrees, references, ISO/IEC 17025:2017 accreditation cert + audit reports; ISO-compliant processes; maintain ISO accreditation for all ARM 42.39.614 methods/analytes at all times. Verify: whether old pending-accreditation pathway (written evidence + inspection indicating accreditation within 12 months, former 37.107.302 material) survives — NOT confirmed.
  • ARM 42.39.602 (Cornell; NEW 2019, AMD 2022, TRANS from 37.107.302 2023): full-time Scientific Director per §16-12-206; director duties incl. ISO maintained, QA plan, trained/competent personnel, approved SOPs reviewed/followed, method validations (accuracy, precision, linearity/working range, r², LOD, LOQ, MDL, reproducibility), QA/QC programmes, corrective/preventative actions, approved PT programme for all required methods/analytes, adequate physical/environmental conditions, safety/biohazard. Director physically present majority of testing time. Lab must be able to perform ≥75% of QA testing requirements in-house (Cornell cross-ref to stale ARM 37.107.316 — Verify, should be 42.39.614). Referral only to another licensed MT lab meeting subchapter; referred lab identified in all reports/CoAs/seed-to-sale; written licensee permission before sending sample elsewhere. Certification in CoA; data/reports incl. QC, curves, raw data, calculations, spreadsheets, CoAs, licensee reports retained ≥3 years on premises, readily available. Written emergency procedures (fire, chemical spill, other).
  • ARM 42.39.614 (Cornell opened): all items for final sale/transfer tested in final form; addition of any ingredient/reagent after final QA testing requires retesting for mandatory QA (QA Testing Requirements Appendix v1.0, 24 Aug 2024, adopted by reference; copy via DOR www.mtrevenue.gov/cannabis and 125 N. Roberts St, Helena). Composite lab test samples from same/different process lots and test batches prohibited; composite from same/different harvest lots and test batches strictly prohibited; effective 24 Feb 2025 multi-strain harvest lots and multi-strain composite lab samples prohibited. Usable marijuana: all harvest lots/test batches Table 1.0a before final sale and before use in pre-rolls/direct infused products; usable incl. trim/manicure may be left untested only if subjected to solvent/non-solvent extraction for concentrates/extracts. Concentrates/extracts: all process lots/test batches Table 1.0b before final sale and before use in infused products/pre-rolls. Infused products: Table 1.0c before final sale. Pre-rolls: Table 1.0d if potency expected to change from previously tested compliant usable (e.g. mixing strains; all infused pre-rolls). Items combining ≥2 previously tested compliant items (moon rocks, canna-cigars): potency before final sale. No use of untested item in infused products/pre-rolls; no use of untested item (except usable incl. trim/manicure) in concentrates/extracts. Potency per sample: THCA, THC, Total THC, CBDA, CBD + all other cannabinoids reported on CoA, entered in seed-to-sale.
  • Failure thresholds in opened Cornell text: moisture >15.0% — CONFLICT: earlier Esper staging-mirror snippet said >12.0%; check current rules.mt.gov. Filth/foreign matter: >5.0% stems ≥3mm diameter, or >2.0% seeds/other foreign matter. Microbiological: Salmonella non-detectable in 1.0g; STEC non-detectable in 1.0g; pathogenic Aspergillus A. flavus, A. fumigatus, A. niger, A. terreus each non-detectable in 1.0g; molecular methods must include enrichment step. Mycotoxin: total aflatoxin B1/B2/G1/G2 20 µg/kg; ochratoxin A 20 µg/kg. Residual solvents: > Table 2.0 action levels. Heavy metals: tested at random; failure > Table 3.0. Pesticides: > Tables 4.0a/4.0b as of respective effective dates.
  • NOT obtained: Appendix Tables 1.0a–1.0d, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0a, 4.0b; DOR testing-lab page points to Appendix PDF, direct URL not captured — Verify/obtain.
  • Proposed, NOT law: MAR 2025-372.1 would amend ARM 42.39.601/610/614 + SOP-001 v3.0 to increase acceptable test-batch size; adoption/effective date not confirmed — Verify, do not present larger batch size as law.
  • Do NOT use: FoodHandlersGuide page claiming DPHHS oversight, pesticide 0.00 ppm, microbial 0.20 ppm, solvents 0.10 ppm, heavy metals 0.30 ppm, mycotoxins 0.002 ppm — stale/wrong for CARD regime.
  • ARM 42.39.610 sampling (Cornell; NEW 2022, TRANS from 37.107.310 2023, AMD 2024): lab must collect samples; sampling SOP incl. random/representative method for all matrices, sterile technique, sampler training, equipment, transport/storage, custody/integrity, documentation/records. Sampling report form per event per SOP-001: licensee name/ID, physical address, sampler badge ID, lab vehicle/plate, route begin/end date/time. Sample integrity in transport/storage; store as label directs. Licensee employee present to observe, must not assist sampler physically collecting. Sample random/representative, meets SOP-001, sufficient for all QA incl. QC/re-runs. All increments from same test batch into one container free of analytes of interest, appropriate for analysis. ≥50% of lab test sample homogenized before analysis. Refuse collection if adulteration suspected, report to Dept within 3 business days (kief application, solvents application, deleterious substance to evade results, substance to increase cannabinoid result, substance to alter weight). Licensee only orders tests for items it grew/produced/processed or legally purchased from another licensee. Licensee stores/secures test batches maintaining integrity/security during testing. Failed/remediated items re-tested at same lab unless Dept grants explicit written permission otherwise. Dept may inspect sampling events. SOP-001 v3.0 (24 Aug 2024) adopted by reference in (14); copy via DOR + Helena address. GAP: SOP-001 numeric batch/lot size tables not opened — do not state numeric batch sizes without opening SOP-001.
  • Untested/failed disposition: sale bar §16-12-209(1); ARM 42.39.614(9) untested-use bars (above). Failed samples §16-12-209(9) collect/destroy except THC-only excess. Retest §16-12-209(7) + ARM 42.39.610(12) same-lab rule. Whether failed batch automatically quarantined in Metrc pending remediation/destruction beyond §16-12-209(9)/610(12) — Verify vs current ARM 42.39.614/203 + QA Appendix. Seed-to-sale quarantine for packages under administrative action in ARM 42.39.203 as amended eff. 22 Aug 2026 (Run 1 anchor, MAR 2026-445.2).
  • Enforcement illustration, search-result only, Verify before summarising beyond these facts: Nordic Labs LLC, Testing Lab Licence L100440-001, License Modification Agreement (URL as returned incl. apparent space/truncation — verify exact URL before linking: revenuefiles.mt.gov/files/Cannabis/Currently-Suspende-Licenses/Nordic-labs-LLC.pdf). DOR audited 11 Apr 2025 re pesticide method; 15 Apr 2025 told Nordic to cease pesticide analysis until certain conditions; 17 Apr 2025 Laboratory Audit Report finding 4 deficiencies, all pesticide-related (unvalidated method used — PJLA-approved validated method provided to DOR was not method used on-site; data studies on method effects + updated validation study not conducted; Scientific Director unable to ensure ISO 17025:2017 compliance because method not sufficiently validated). Conditions incl. method validation with new extraction procedure to DOR + PJLA, ARM 42.39.602(2)(h) requirements, Measurement Uncertainty per ISO 17025:2017 §7.6; updated SOP with all extraction steps + transition table incl. parent/daughter ions + retention times to DOR + PJLA; on-site DOR audit for pesticide analysis. DOR agreed not to pursue formal summary suspension under §16-12-210(7) so long as licensee complied. Full agreement not opened.

11(b) Security

  • ARM 42.39.121 (Cornell; NEW 2021, eff. 1 Jan 2022; auth. §16-12-112; impl. §§16-12-101/112/202/207/222): written proactive security plan on premises safeguarding vs theft/diversion/tampering on premises + during transit if applicable; minimum policies: restrict access to areas containing marijuana/products to authorized persons only; identify authorized persons in those areas; electronic monitoring. Single secure entrance for public ingress/egress. Security alarm on all perimeter entry points + perimeter windows as applicable. Video cameras: min 640x470 pixels or pixel equivalent analog; record continuously 24 hrs/day or motion-sensor, min 10 fps; placed for optimal identification of any person/activities in limited access areas; placed to record all entrances/exits to indoor facility from indoor + outdoor vantage points. Keep recordings min 60 days; clear/accurate date/time display. Videos subject to Dept inspection, copy on request. (3)–(6) not required until 1 Apr 2022.
  • Proposed, NOT law: MAR 2026-129.1 proposes amending ARM 42.39.121 re video recording formats, adding new (5)(c) specifying acceptable formats under §16-12-207(10)(b) ("capable of being recorded as prescribed by the department"); hearing requests due Mon 24 Aug 2026 5pm; notice estimated 354 affected active licensees; adoption/effective date not confirmed — Verify.
  • ARM 42.39.105 as amended MAR 2026-155.2 eff. 26 Sep 2026 (adoption notice opened; 2026 MAR Issue No. 15; amending ARM 42.39.102/105 re day-to-day operational control — resolves Run 1 MAR 2026-155 numbering in favour of Parts 4–5 track, but cite final register text): employees wear Dept-issued worker permit clearly visible on outermost clothing while working. Licensee responsible for security of all marijuana/products on premises, during transit, under licensee/employee supervision until sold. Licensee provides Dept hours of operation per premises; licensee/employee on-site during hours, premises available to inspectors during hours. Weighing device per §30-12-203 + ARM 24.351.101 whenever marijuana weighed (full sentence truncated in search result — Verify). Written emergency procedures (fire, chemical spill, other) established/maintained/available for inspection at all premises. Single binder/file/organized readily accessible format for inspection: proof signage complies with local sign ordinances; proof licensees/employees current on required trainings; emergency procedures; written security plan per ARM 42.39.121(1); manufacturers: written SOPs per ARM 42.39.401(16); cultivators: SOPs per ARM 42.39.405(8); dispensaries: written SOP for destruction of returned marijuana/products.
  • ARM 42.39.413 transport security (Cornell; NEW 2021, AMD 2022, AMD 2024; auth. §§16-12-112/222): transporter licence allows physical distribution/delivery to licensed premises + to registered cardholders in MT presenting valid registry ID. Applicants: proof of valid MT driver's licence. All distribution/delivery: in motor vehicle as defined by ARM 42.39.102; depart from licensed premises, deliver to licensed premises or cardholder address (cardholder provides valid registry ID before transport completed); accompanied by seed-to-sale transport manifest (departure address/licence no., arrival address/licence or cardholder no., actual departure date/estimated time, actual arrival date/estimated time, transporter driver's licence no., vehicle make/model/plate, name/signature of each accompanying licensee/employee, complete product description incl. name/type, accurate amount verified by count or weighing device per §30-12-203 + ARM 24.351.101, RFID tag numbers); within 7 days from departure. Overnight stay on planned direct route: identify stay on manifest beforehand, accept in-transit product into storage facility inventory or other licensed premises, store in licensee's licensed premises. Record actual departure/arrival in seed-to-sale; manifest may not be voided/changed after leaving departure; copy to each receiving premises. Receiving premises prohibited from receiving without valid manifest; on receipt ensure product as described verified by count/weighing device, immediately record receipt, document differences, immediately report discrepancies to Dept. While in transport: shielded from public view, secured in locked storage compartment inside vehicle body. Transport vehicles: considered licensed premises for Dept inspection; may be stopped/inspected at any licensed premises or during transport; lockable + security alarm; SHALL NOT leave Montana while any amount of marijuana/product in vehicle; no external markings/words/symbols indicating marijuana transport or marijuana business ownership/lease. Transporter/employee must not sell product or transport directly to consumers (textual basis, with MCA §16-12-222(7), for adult-use consumer-delivery prohibition for transporters). Contact Dept within 24 hrs if vehicle in accident involving product loss. Manifest + delivery receipts presented to law enforcement/authorized Dept employees on request. If transporter maintains licensed premises to temporarily store product, needs storage facility endorsement per ARM 42.39.420 per facility.
  • ARM 42.39.420 (Cornell; NEW 2021; auth. §§16-12-112/222): transporter or testing lab shall obtain storage facility endorsement for any overnight storage; separate off-site facility; temporary storage only, ≤48 hours; may not grow/process/test/manufacture/consume/sell there; may not share with any other marijuana business; only in §16-12-301 jurisdiction.
  • Hemp-specific security: Run 1 negative finding re-affirmed for this round — no hemp-specific security rule located in ARM Title 4 Ch. 19 or MDA hemp guidance opened in Run 1; ARM 42.39.121/413 regime by its terms applies to marijuana licensees. Verify vs current ARM Title 4 Ch. 19 before stating as final negative.
  • CCD Apr 2022 newsletter (official guidance, dated — do not treat as current rule text; https://revenue.mt.gov/news/2022/cannabis-control-division-april-newsletter, 5 Apr 2022): video system must at minimum allow transmission/storage by digital means displaying interior + exterior, capable of being recorded as prescribed by Dept; all ARM 42.39.121 provisions to be in place by 1 Apr 2022. Minors not allowed in dispensary unless registered cardholder; cardholder must be accompanied by parent/legal guardian. No sale/transfer through drive-up window EXCEPT dispensary may hand-deliver to registered cardholder in vehicle parked immediately outside subject dispensary. All purchased products placed into exit package — sealed bag — before customer leaves. Updated inspector checklist encouraged; current version not obtained — Verify/obtain for inspection-frequency/item detail.

11(c) Retail operations

  • MCA §16-12-224 (MCA 2025 text, search result; official mca.legmt.gov page; hist. En. §5 Ch. 576 L. 2021, amended thereafter — full history line truncated, Verify on leg.mt.gov): (4) medical dispensary sells exclusively to registered cardholders: marijuana, products, live plants. (5) adult-use dispensary sells marijuana, products, live plants to consumers or cardholders. (6) fee $5,000 first location (adult-use or medical), cumulatively +$5,000 per additional location under same licence. (8)(a) sold on THC concentration basis, not weight. (8)(b) single package limits (except (8)(d)): (i) flower 1 oz usable, total potential psychoactive THC ≤35%; (ii) capsule ≤100 mg/capsule, ≤800 mg/package; (iii) tincture ≤800 mg; (iv) edible/food ≤100 mg/package, single serving ≤5 mg (MCA 2025 text consistent with HB 636 eff. 1 Jul 2026; older 10 mg in 2021 Justia text superseded — do not use); (v) topical ≤6% THC, ≤800 mg/package; (vi) suppository/transdermal ≤100 mg/unit, ≤800 mg/package; (vii) any other ≤800 mg. (8)(c) 10% deviation above/below allowed under (8)(b)(ii)–(vii). (8)(d) dispensary may sell higher THC potency than (8) to registered cardholders.
  • (9) RESOLVES Run 1 per-transaction vs per-day question for adult-use: "A licensee or employee is prohibited from conducting a transaction that would result in a consumer or registered cardholder exceeding the personal possession amounts set forth in 16-12-106 and 16-12-515." Therefore NO separate adult-use per-day purchase cap in §16-12-224; transaction test = whether that transaction pushes buyer over personal possession amount (adult: 1 oz usable, ≤8 g concentrate, ≤800 mg THC solid edibles within 1 oz — Run 1 anchor). Medical per-day/per-month (≤1 oz/day, ≤5 oz/month, possession 1 oz — §16-12-515) is Part 5 medical rule, not adult-use daily cap; secondary "5 oz possession" remains WRONG.
  • Remaining Verify: whether current ARM dispensary rules impose additional per-transaction tracking duty (e.g. Metrc sale entry at point of sale, ID-scan/record retention) beyond §16-12-224(9) + ARM 42.39.203. 2021 NEW RULE IV ID-checking material stale, not relied on. DOR "Purchasing Power and Identification Requirements at a Dispensary" guidance cited secondarily but not opened — Verify/open before stating ID-checking procedure as current practice.
  • Delivery resolution: transporter delivery cardholder-only (ARM 42.39.413(1), (3)(b); (13) must not transport directly to consumers) — adult-use consumer delivery by transporter prohibited, consistent with Run 1 MCA §16-12-222(7). Dispensary-own delivery (vs transporter delivery) remains Verify: no current ARM dispensary rule text opened affirmatively authorising or prohibiting dispensary's own employees delivering to cardholder or consumer; only dispensary-delivery-adjacent authority found is Apr 2022 newsletter hand-delivery exception for cardholder in vehicle parked immediately outside subject dispensary. Do not state dispensaries may/may not deliver beyond that exception without opening current ARM dispensary rules.
  • Signage ARM 42.39.122 (Cornell; NEW 2021, eff. 1 Jan 2022; auth. §16-12-112; impl. §§16-12-101/112/203/207): (1) conspicuously display + make available site identification certificate at each premises. (2) All licensees except dispensaries conspicuously post at each entry: "Persons under twenty-one years of age not permitted on these premises without a valid worker permit." (3) All dispensaries conspicuously post at each entry: (a) "Persons under twenty-one years of age not permitted on these premises without a valid registry identification card or valid worker permit. Registered cardholders under the age of 18 must be accompanied by a legal guardian." (b) "No on-site consumption of marijuana or marijuana products." (c) "No on-site opening of marijuana or marijuana products." (4) Medical-only wording: licensee not permitted/electing not to sell adult-use must conspicuously post at entry, visible from outside: "This dispensary is licensed for medical marijuana sales only. No sales will be permitted without a valid registry identification card." Verify: brief refers to ARM 42.39.122 "as amended 2026" — Cornell version opened shows only 2021 NEW history, no 2026 amendment line; confirm any 2026 amendment on rules.mt.gov/MAR 2026 before stating any 2026 signage change. Point-of-sale poster, exit packaging (child-resistant + opaque, state warnings, state QR code), ARM 42.39.314–320 labelling beyond Run 1 summary not re-opened in full; Run 1 anchors carry (ARM 42.39.319 exit packaging; eff. 22 Aug 2026, MAR 2026-93.2, HB 792). Local sign-ordinance interaction via ARM 42.39.105 binder duty + 500-ft same-street buffer §16-12-207(3)(a)(iii) + ARM 42.39.124 three-part test (Run 1 anchors).
  • Hours/entrance/seed-to-sale (Run 1 anchors carried): dispensary hours 9am–8pm, single secured entrance (ARM 42.39.121(2) confirms single-entrance rule in current text). Seed-to-sale Metrc; ARM 42.39.203 as amended eff. 22 Aug 2026 (primary inventory system, UID tags, QA samples recorded, compliance notifications resolved within 30 days, quarantine of packages under administrative action; DOR Sep 2026 newsletter confirms daily inventory reconciliation by 11:59pm + harvest recording in Metrc — https://revenue.mt.gov/news/ccd-archives/september-2026-cannabis-newsletter).

11(d) Compliance / enforcement

  • Discipline MCA §16-12-109 (official MCA 2025 page opened; I-190 §20, Ch. 576, Ch. 712): (1) if Dept has reasonable cause to believe licensee violated chapter or Dept rule, in discretion + in addition to other penalties: (a) reprimand; (b) revoke; (c) suspend up to 1 year; (d) refuse renewal after expiration; (e) civil penalty ≤$3,000. (2) Mitigating Dept shall consider / may use to adjust within ranges: compliance prior 3 years; good faith efforts to prevent violation; cooperation in investigation + acceptance of responsibility by licensee/employee/agent. (3) Aggravating: prior warnings; prior violations past 3 years; lack of written policies governing employee conduct; additional violations revealed during investigation; efforts to conceal; intentional violations; involvement of >1 patron/employee. (4) For each licensing programme, Dept designated criminal justice agency within §44-5-103 for confidential criminal justice info re licensees/applicants + possible unlicensed practice. (5) Dept SHALL revoke + may not reissue licence/endorsement belonging to person: (a) whose CBO is individual convicted of felony drug offense; (b) who allows another person not authorized/lawfully allowed to be in possession of licence; (c) who transports marijuana/products outside Montana unless otherwise allowed by federal law; (d) who operates CO2/hydrocarbon extraction without manufacturing licence; (e) who purchases marijuana from unauthorized source in violation of chapter; (f) who sells/distributes/transfers to person licensee knows/should know is under 21 unless registered cardholder. (6) Revoked licensee may not reapply for 3 years from revocation. (7)(a) Review of Dept action imposing fine/suspension/revocation as contested case hearing before Dept office of dispute resolution under Montana APA. (7)(b) Appeal of Dept decision re issuance/rejection/suspension/revocation to district court in county where person operates/proposes to operate (or any such county if multiple). (7)(c) Appeal by filing complaint setting forth grounds + relief demanded with district court within 30 days following receipt of notice of Dept final decision. GAP: pre-hearing notice procedure (form, timing, summary-suspension under §16-12-210(7) referenced in Nordic agreement) not opened in full — Verify §§16-12-210 + ARM Title 42 Ch. 2 before stating notice periods.
  • Inspections: ARM 42.39.121(6) videos subject to inspection, copy on request; ARM 42.39.413(12)(a) transport vehicles are licensed premises for inspection, may be stopped/inspected at any licensed premises or during transport; ARM 42.39.610(13) Dept may inspect sampling events; ARM 42.39.105 premises available to inspectors during hours, binder available. NO inspection-frequency rule located in texts opened — Unavailable on current evidence, do not state frequency. Current CCD inspector checklist not obtained. Cannabis Laboratory Program lab inspections: beyond ARM 42.39.610(13) + Nordic audit example, no separate frequency/programme document opened — Verify vs DOR Cannabis Laboratory Program materials.
  • Recalls ARM 42.39.312 as amended MAR 2026-447.2 eff. 11 Jul 2026: proposed MAR 2026-447.1 published 8 May 2026 (2026 MAR Issue No. 9); public hearing 1 Jun 2026 (2 industry-association reps present, no testimony, no written comments); final action eff. 11 Jul 2026, amended as proposed. Source adoption PDF https://revenuefiles.mt.gov/files/Posts/rules-hearings/MAR-No-2026-447-2.pdf — search-result only, full amended text not opened — Verify verbatim. DOR Sep 2026 newsletter confirms revised rule eff. 11 Jul 2026, summary of key changes to recall plan requirements, notification procedures, timelines. Secondary (Marijuana Herald 1 Sep 2026, https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/09/montana-implements-new-marijuana-labeling-tracking-and-recall-rules/ — NOT rule text): most businesses maintain written auditable recall plan identifying recall coordinator + procedures for isolating, retrieving, destroying, communicating about affected products. Recalls may be required when business cannot prove product satisfied testing, product failed testing + not successfully remediated/retested, or testing conducted improperly/invalid. Once recall begins: activate plan within 24 hours, press release/other public notification within 48 hours, weekly progress reports, destroy or when allowed remediate within 30 business days; failure may result in administrative action. ALL timelines Verify vs adopted ARM 42.39.312 before stating as law. Bloomberg Tax 12 May 2026 on proposal (secondary, paywalled summary): amendments clarify primary party responsible when multiple licensees involved, specify trigger events, establish mandatory timelines, enhance documentation/tracking, add explicit enforcement mechanisms; apply to cultivators, manufacturers, dispensaries, testing labs — not substitute for rule. Recall hub status (Run 1 anchor, re-check note): hub incomplete in Run 1 — on-hub S&J Strategic Enterprises LLC 28 Jan 2026, Broughton Inc. 7 Apr 2025; off-hub PDFs Hometree Co. 12 Feb 2026, A Lucker LLC 11 Sep 2025, Selective Strains LLC 20 May 2025. Live re-check for new recalls after Run 1 not completed — Verify live before publishing recall list. No standalone public enforcement/disciplinary list found in Run 1 or this round; Nordic agreement + Broughton order are only enforcement illustrations, neither is a list.
  • Enforcement case Broughton Inc. v. City and County of Butte-Silver Bow, No. 2:25-cv-00065-BMM (D. Mont.): CourtListener PDF Doc. 28 Order on Motion for Summary Judgment filed 12 Mar 2026, http://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.mtd.81186/gov.uscourts.mtd.81186.28.0.pdf — search-result excerpt only, full 21-page order NOT read end-to-end, Verify before quoting any holding; GovInfo docket https://www.govinfo.gov/app/details/USCOURTS-mtd-2_25-cv-00065. Excerpt establishes without over-reading: plaintiff Broughton Inc., MT corporation, owns "Grommit LLC DBA Starrbuds"; Glenn + Jill Broughton own Broughton; applied for business licence for Starrbuds, proposed retail cannabis dispensary at 301 South Arizona Ave, Butte. 23 Apr 2025 Building Dept contact (Christensen) emailed Jill Broughton licence "approved and ready to issue", Building Dept inspected/approved location as complying with building codes. BSB staff then analysed proximity to Butte High School: entrances to welding classroom + proposed dispensary; County Attorney (Enrooth) agreed with staff (Crain) location, same street as school entrance + within 500 ft of school, prohibited BSB from approving business licence under §16-12-207. 27 May 2025 meeting parties disagreed on §16-12-207 interpretation; Broughton counsel wrote 28 May 2025 requesting licence or notice of refusal by 9 Jun 2025. BSB moved summary judgment (Doc. 14); Broughton opposed (Doc. 18); hearing 2 Feb 2026 (Doc. 24); Order filed 12 Mar 2026 (Doc. 28). Excerpt's summary of reasoning: Broughton's Fifth Amendment takings + Fourteenth Amendment substantive due process claims fail as matter of law, on ground (as framed in excerpt) Broughton lacked vested property interest where BSB had not issued business licence + state/local requirements incl. §16-12-207 proximity not met. Do NOT over-read: distance figure (~475 ft, prior round) + full disposition of every claim not re-verified from full order. Case is local-licensing / §16-12-207 proximity dispute, not ruling dispensary was/was not compliant on merits of state licence, not DOR enforcement action. Butte-Silver Bow ≠ Anaconda-Deer Lodge; city of Deer Lodge (Powell County) ≠ Deer Lodge County (Anaconda) — neither involved here.

11(e) Banking / payments (secondary only — label as such)

  • No federal cannabis banking law (baseline). No MT statute located creating state cannabis-banking safe harbour; do not present one.
  • Clearwater Credit Union (Missoula): Run 1 secondary (2022) deposit-only, no lending. No 2026 terms located this round — do not present 2022 terms as current; 2026 terms Unavailable.
  • Herring Bank MT Cannabis Banking page dated 18 Oct 2026 (secondary marketing): https://www.herringbank.com/business-banking/cannabis-banking/montana/ — claims MT cannabis/CBD banking incl. 24/7 online banking, armored courier cash transport, ACH/wire, payroll, electronic tax payments, FDIC-insured accounts, connection to armored courier, services for licensed dispensaries + cultivation. Marketing claims, not verified terms/fees/eligibility — label as such. Separate Herring SAFE Banking Act page describes SAFE Banking Act (reintroduced 119th Congress as SAFE Banking Act of 2026) as pending, not enacted: https://www.herringbank.com/business-banking/cannabis-banking/safe-banking-act/.
  • Safe Harbor Financial FAQ: "Safe Harbor is a financial technology company, not a bank"; deposits held by federally insured partner banks/credit unions, FDIC/NCUA protected up to $250,000 per depositor per ownership category; services in 41 US states/territories incl. Montana. Source http://shfinancial.org/about-us/faq — secondary, provider's own FAQ, not MT regulator source.
  • Practical consequence to state plainly: no federal banking law → MT licensees' banking access depends on individual institutions' BSA/AML programmes + FinCEN guidance, not MT banking entitlement. Card-network acceptance, cash handling, lending terms not verified for any named institution this round.

PART 12: CRIMINAL LAW, EXPUNGEMENT, DRIVING, FIREARMS, OTHER CONSEQUENCES

12(a) Criminal law above Chapter 12 limits — gatekeeping

Title 45 Ch. 9 offences gated by "Except as provided in Title 16, chapter 12" + (possession/intent) "in an amount greater than permitted or for which a penalty is not specified under Title 16, chapter 12." Within-limit Ch. 12 conduct is NOT a Title 45 Ch. 9 offence; above-limit/unlicensed is. §16-12-108(8) (Run 1 anchor): Title 45 Ch. 9 applies unless exempted by Ch. 12. Maxima below must never be presented as applying to within-limit possession/home grow/licensed activity. Run 1 possession anchors: adult 1 oz usable (≤8g concentrate, ≤800mg THC solid edibles, within 1 oz); adult home grow 2 mature + 2 seedlings/person, 4+4/residence; medical home grow 4+4; medical purchase ≤1 oz/day, ≤5 oz/month; possession 1 oz. 1–2 oz tier is Ch. 12 civil tier (forfeiture + ≤$200/4 hrs community service 1st, ≤$300/6 hrs 2nd, ≤$500/8 hrs 3rd+); visible-from-public-place + unlocked-space: civil fine ≤$250 + forfeiture; smoking in public ≤$50.

  • §45-9-102 criminal possession of dangerous drugs (official MCA 2025 page opened; En. §5 Ch. 314 L. 1969; …; amd. §42 I.M. 190; §70 Ch. 576 L. 2021): (1) "Except as provided in Title 16, chapter 12, or 50-32-609, a person commits the offense of criminal possession of dangerous drugs if the person possesses any dangerous drug, as defined in 50-32-101, [in an amount] greater than permitted or for which a penalty is not specified under Title 16, chapter 12." (2) state prison ≤5 years or fine ≤$5,000 or both. (3) first violation presumed entitled to deferred imposition of sentence of imprisonment. (4) ultimate users/practitioners (§50-32-101) + supervised agents in professional practice exempt. Superseded secondary — do NOT use: pre-2021 <60g / 1g hashish first-offense $100–$500 + up to 6 months county jail, second up to 3 yrs/$1,000, >60g felony up to 5 yrs/$50,000. Current §45-9-102 has no 60-gram tier, $5,000 (not $50,000) fine cap. NORML current table (1–2 oz civil; >2 oz felony 5 yrs/$45,000; >16g concentrate felony 5 yrs/$50,000) conflicts with current §45-9-102 on fine figure, must not be quoted without flagging conflict. 1–2 oz tier is civil under Ch. 12, not §45-9-102 felony; only amounts above Ch. 12 civil tier / beyond permitted amounts fall to §45-9-102, statute as opened states no weight threshold of its own.
  • §45-9-103 possession with intent to distribute (official MCA 2025 opened; …; §43 I.M. 190; §71 Ch. 576; §2 Ch. 543 L. 2023): (1) "Except as provided in Title 16, chapter 12, a person commits the offense of criminal possession with intent to distribute if the person possesses with intent to distribute any dangerous drug as defined in 50-32-101 in an amount greater than permitted or for which a penalty is not specified under Title 16, chapter 12." (2) except (3): state prison ≤20 years or fine ≤$50,000 or both. (3) fentanyl-specific: ≥2 yrs ≤40 yrs or fine ≤$50,000 or both; court may not suspend/defer first 2 yrs except §46-18-222(1)–(4); not eligible for parole first 2 yrs. (4) practitioners/supervised agents exempt.
  • §45-9-101 criminal distribution (official MCA 2025 opened; …; §41 I.M. 190; §69 Ch. 576; §1 Ch. 543 L. 2023): (1) "Except as provided in Title 16, chapter 12, a person commits the offense of criminal distribution of dangerous drugs if the person sells, barters, exchanges, gives away, or offers to sell, barter, exchange, or give away any dangerous drug, as defined in 50-32-101." (2) distribution involving giving away/sharing sentenced as §45-9-102 (5-yr/$5,000 max, not 25-yr). (3) distribution not otherwise in (1)(2)(4)(5)(6): state prison ≤25 yrs or fine ≤$50,000 or both. (4) adult distribution to minor: first state prison ≤40 yrs, fine ≤$50,000; second/subsequent state prison ≤life, fine ≤$50,000. (5) offense results in death from use of distributed drug: state prison ≤100 yrs, fine ≤$100,000. (6) fentanyl/carfentanil/sufentanil/alfentanil/fentanyl-derivative distribution where person possessed/distributed mixture >100 pills or >10g combined (powder/solid/liquid incl. additives/cutting agents): ≥2 yrs ≤40 yrs or fine ≤$50,000 or both; may not suspend/defer first 2 yrs except §46-18-222(1)–(4); no parole first 2 yrs. (7) practitioners/supervised agents exempt.
  • §45-9-110 criminal production/manufacture (official MCA 2025 opened; En. §1 Ch. 448 L. 1993; …; §44 I.M. 190; §72 Ch. 576): (1) "Except as provided in Title 16, chapter 12, a person commits the offense of criminal production or manufacture of dangerous drugs if the person knowingly or purposely produces, manufactures, prepares, cultivates, compounds, or processes a dangerous drug, as defined in 50-32-101." (2) general max: state prison ≤25 yrs, fine ≤$50,000. (3) Marijuana/THC-specific tier (governs above-limit home grow + unlicensed manufacture): "A person convicted of production of marijuana or tetrahydrocannabinol in an amount greater than permitted or for which a penalty is not specified under Title 16, chapter 12, or manufacture without the appropriate license pursuant to Title 16, chapter 12, shall be imprisoned in the state prison for a term of not more than 5 years and may be fined an amount not to exceed $5,000, except that if the total weight is more than a pound or the number of plants is more than 30, the person shall be imprisoned in the state prison for a term of not more than 25 years and may be fined an amount not to exceed $50,000. 'Weight' means the weight of the dry plant and includes the leaves and stem structure but does not include the root structure." (4) practitioners/supervised agents exempt. Superseded — do NOT use: State v. Rathbun, 2003 MT 210, then-max 10 yrs/$50,000 under §45-9-110(4) is pre-2021, superseded by current (3). Verify: §45-9-130 (mandatory fine 35% of market value, at issue in State v. Le, 2017, Dawson County, 23 lbs marijuana) not checked for current applicability — do not state it applies to marijuana after I-190/HB 701 without opening current §45-9-130. §45-7-307 cross-ref in FindLaw summary of §16-12-108 (violation of (1)(h)(iv) subject to §45-7-307; violation of (1)(n) solvent extraction subject to §45-9-110(3)) not in Run 1 §16-12-108 excerpt — Verify vs current §16-12-108 before including.
  • Paraphernalia §45-10-103 (MCA 2025 archive page in search results; En. §3 Ch. 481 L. 1981; …; §46 I.M. 190; §75 Ch. 576; §1 Ch. 723 L. 2023): "Except as provided in Title 16, chapter 12, or 50-32-609, it is unlawful for a person to use or to possess with intent to use drug paraphernalia to plant, propagate, cultivate, grow, harvest, manufacture, compound, convert, produce, process, prepare, analyze, pack, repack, store, contain, conceal, inject, ingest, inhale, or otherwise introduce into the human body a dangerous drug. A person who violates this section is guilty of a misdemeanor and upon conviction shall be imprisoned in the county jail for not more than 6 months, fined an amount of not more than $500, or both. A person convicted of a first violation of this section is presumed to be entitled to a deferred imposition of sentence of imprisonment." Interaction (Run 1): §16-12-106(1)(e) paraphernalia possession/transfer lawful for 18+ in Ch. 12 context; §16-12-106(6) under-18 non-cardholder paraphernalia consequences (forfeiture + 8 hrs drug education/counselling). MSU DAAPP (May 2026) summary stating under-18 paraphernalia is "in violation of 45-10-103" should be flagged vs §16-12-106(6) rather than adopted silently.
  • Synthetic cannabinoids + Schedule I tetrahydrocannabinols §50-32-222 (official MCA 2025 page opened; full page 334 lines; operative (4)(x), (4)(ff), (4)(kk)): (4)(x) "marijuana" listed as Schedule I hallucinogenic substance (subject to "unless specifically excepted or listed in another schedule" headnote — why Ch. 12 lawful conduct is not Schedule I offence in practice, and why federal Schedule III medical move does not by itself rewrite MT Schedule I listing). (4)(ff) "tetrahydrocannabinols, neutral compounds, and their corresponding acids, including synthetic equivalents of the substances contained in the plant or in the resinous extractives of cannabis, or synthetic substances, derivatives, and their isomers with similar chemical structure and pharmacological activity, such as those listed in (4)(ff)(i)–(iii)" — incl. (i) delta 9 (delta 1) cis/trans + optical isomers; (ii) delta 8 (delta 6) cis/trans + optical isomers; (iii) delta 6a,10a (delta 3,4) cis/trans + optical isomers. (4)(kk) "synthetic cannabinoids" incl. (i) any chemical compound chemically synthesized from or structurally similar to listed groups (naphthoylindoles, naphthylmethylindoles, naphthoylpyrroles, naphthylmethylindenes, acetylindoles, cyclohexylphenols, dibenzopyrans, benzoylindoles, salts/isomers/analogs); (ii) any compound demonstrated to have agonist binding activity at ≥1 cannabinoid receptors or chemical analog/isomer; named compounds (iii) JWH-018, (iv) HU-210, (v) CP-47,497 + homologues, (vi) JWH-073, (vii) JWH-200, (viii) JWH-250, (ix) JWH-019, (x) JWH-398, (xi) JWH-081, (xii) WIN-55,212-2, HU-243, named acetate compound. Ch. 12 prohibitions (Run 1 verbatim): §16-12-108(1)(d) Ch. 12 does not permit production/delivery/distribution/purchase/consumption of synthetic cannabinoid products; §16-12-117(1) person may not manufacture/process/store/offer for sale synthetic cannabinoid or product (En. Ch. 746 L. 2023 HB 948; amd. Ch. 100 L. 2025 HB 49). Consequence: synthetic cannabinoid Schedule I under §50-32-222(4)(kk) + not Ch. 12-permitted falls to Title 45 Ch. 9 (possession §45-9-102, intent §45-9-103, distribution §45-9-101, manufacture §45-9-110) on same gatekeeping logic. No separate synthetic-only criminal penalty section located; do not invent one. Delta-8 note: delta-8 THC expressly Schedule I under §50-32-222(4)(ff)(ii). Sits alongside 2025 hemp restrictions (HB 49 / SB 375 framework Run 1 / Part 7–10: hemp products outside licensed channel limited to 0.5 mg delta-9/serving, 2 mg/package under HB 49, with SB 375 barring sale of hemp products containing delta-9 THC to consumers unless FDA-authorized as food/drug — secondary summary LegalClarity, accessed 1 Nov 2026, https://legalclarity.org/is-delta-9-thc-legal-to-buy-in-montana/; Verify both bills' enrolled text before stating hemp retail rule in final HTML). Do not conflate Schedule I listing with hemp definition: hemp ≤0.3% total delta-9 THC dry-weight is hemp under §80-18-101 (Run 1), not marijuana; above threshold is marijuana under §16-12-102(20)/§80-18-101 and Ch. 12 / Title 45 Ch. 9 apply. No separate "hemp above 0.3%" criminal offence located; state reasoning explicitly rather than inventing hemp-specific crime.
  • Under-21 offences — numbering RESOLVED in favour of §45-5-639. Official MCA 2025 page opened: https://mca.legmt.gov/bills/mca/title_0450/chapter_0050/part_0060/section_0390/0450-0050-0060-0390.html; hist. En. §2 Ch. 155 L. 2025. §45-5-639 Possession of or unlawful attempt to purchase marijuana — interference with court order is current marijuana-specific under-21 offence. Older summaries citing §45-5-624 cite alcohol-code numbering; §16-6-314(3) (MCA 2025, amd. §14 Ch. 591 L. 2023; §5 Ch. 155 L. 2025) still cross-refers to "45-5-624(2) or (3), 45-5-638(3) or (4), or 45-5-639(3) or (4)" for under-21 alcohol-code violations — that list is not by itself proof §45-5-624 is marijuana offence. §16-12-106(5) (Run 1, MCA 2025 text) cross-refers under-21 non-cardholder possession/use/delivery to §45-5-639, consistent.
  • Verbatim §45-5-639: (1) person under 21 commits possession of marijuana if knowingly consumes/uses/has in possession/delivers/distributes without consideration marijuana. May not be arrested/charged solely because at place where others possessing/consuming. Does not commit offense if consumes/gains possession because lawfully supplied or when in course of employment necessary to possess. (2)(a) In addition to youth-court disposition under §41-5-1512, person under 18 convicted: (i) first — fine ≥$100 ≤$300; 20 hrs community service; ordered, with parent(s)/guardian, to complete + pay all costs of community-based substance abuse information course meeting (8), if available; if licensed, licence confiscated by court 30 days, except (2)(b); (ii) second — fine $200–$600; 40 hrs; same course duty; licence confiscated 6 months, except (2)(b); chemical dependency assessment + treatment if recommended, per (7); (iii) third/subsequent — fine $300–$900; 60 hrs; same course duty; licence confiscated 6 months, except (2)(b); assessment/treatment if recommended, per (7). (2)(b) If convicted person fails to complete course + has driver's licence, court shall order licence suspended 3 months (first), 9 months (second), 12 months (third/subsequent). (2)(c) Court retains jurisdiction up to 1 year to order suspension under (2)(b). (3) Person 18+ convicted of possession under this section: (a) first — fine $100–$300; 20 hrs; complete/pay for community-based substance abuse information course meeting (8); (b) second — fine $200–$600; 40 hrs; complete/pay for alcohol/drug information course at treatment programme meeting (8), which may in court's discretion + on licensed addiction counselor recommendation include treatment; (c) third/subsequent — fine $300–$900; 60 hrs; same course duty as (b); in court's discretion county jail ≤6 months. (4) Person under 21 commits attempt to purchase marijuana if knowingly attempts to purchase marijuana; convicted fined ≤$150 if under 21 at offense time + may be ordered community service. (5) Defendant currently under 21 who was under 18 when failing to comply with sentence must be transferred to youth court; if failure proceedings in youth court, offender treated as alleged youth in need of intervention (§41-5-103); youth court may enter judgment under §41-5-1512. (6) Interference: person who purposely/knowingly causes child/ward to fail to comply with sentence under this section or youth-court disposition order, on conviction fined $100 or county jail 10 days or both. (7) Second/subsequent possession conviction: chemical dependency assessment ordered, at programme meeting (8), by licensed addiction counselor; person pays if able; assessment describes level of abuse/dependency + recommends treatment level if indicated; person may obtain second assessment at own expense; court orders appropriate treatment level if any, based on counselor determination; counselor notifies court of enrollment + failure to attend. (8) Course/programme approval: community-based substance abuse information course, alcohol/drug information course, chemical dependency assessment must be approved by DPHHS under §53-24-208 or by court or provided under DOC contract, or provided by Title 50 Ch. 5 Pt. 2 hospital providing chemical dependency services + accredited by CMS-approved accrediting entity. (9) Information/statements made by person under 21 to health-care provider or law-enforcement re alleged offense against that person under Title 45 Ch. 5 Pt. 5 may not be used in prosecution of that person under this section; also extends to person who helps victim obtain medical/other assistance or report offense. (10)(a) Medical-amnesty: person under 21 may not be charged/prosecuted under (1) if: (i) person consumed marijuana + seeks medical treatment at health-care facility or contacts law enforcement/EMS for medical treatment; (ii) person accompanies another person under 21 who consumed marijuana + seeks such treatment/contact for other person; (iii) person requires medical treatment as result of consuming marijuana + evidence of violation obtained during seeking/receiving treatment. (10)(b) defines "health care facility" + "medical treatment".

12(b) Expungement / resentencing / redesignation — MCA §16-12-113 (I-190 as amended by HB 701)

Sources: MCA 2025 via LawServer https://www.lawserver.com/law/state/montana/mt-code/montana_code_16-12-113 + ArkLegal PDF https://arklegal.ai/state_statute/1858528/download.pdf?tagged=1; 2020 Justia version for pre-HB 701 comparison https://law.justia.com/codes/montana/2020/title-16/chapter-12/part-1/section-16-12-113/; Montana Courts MMRTA page https://courts.mt.gov/Forms/mmrta; CCResourceCenter MT restoration profile last updated 13 Jun 2026 https://ccresourcecenter.org/state-restoration-profiles/montana-restoration-of-rights-pardon-expungement-sealing/?ref=blog.counselstack.com — all accessed 1 Nov 2026. Hist.: En. §36 I.M. 190, approved 3 Nov 2020; amended HB 701 (2021) Ch. 576 L. 2021. HB 701 §§41 + 48 had retroactive 1 Jan 2021 effective dates for expungement/possession provisions (per MT Supreme Court doc in State v. Maier, DA 21-0218, 23 Sep 2021).

  • (1) "A person currently serving a sentence for an act that is permitted under this chapter or is punishable by a lesser sentence under this chapter than the person was awarded may petition for an expungement of the conviction or resentencing." (2) On receiving petition under (1), expungement/resentencing court (§§16-12-114–116) shall presume petitioner satisfies (1) unless county attorney provides court reasonable basis petitioner does not satisfy criteria. If petitioner satisfies (1), court shall grant unless court determines granting would pose unreasonable risk of danger to public safety. Change from 2020 text: 2020 Justia version required county attorney prove by clear + convincing evidence petitioner did not satisfy criteria; HB 701 lessened burden to "reasonable basis" (confirmed CCResourceCenter 13 Jun 2026). (3) Person serving sentence resentenced under (1) must be given credit for time served + may not be subject to supervision. (4) Resentencing may not result in term longer than original sentence or reinstatement of charges dismissed pursuant to negotiated plea agreement. (5)(a) Person who completed sentence for act permitted under chapter or punishable by lesser sentence than awarded may petition sentencing court to: (i) expunge conviction; or (ii) redesignate conviction as misdemeanor or civil infraction per chapter. (5)(b) Petition must be served on county attorney for county where petition filed. (6) On receiving petition under (5), court shall presume petitioner satisfies (5) unless county attorney provides reasonable basis to contrary. Once applicant satisfies (5), court shall redesignate as misdemeanor/civil infraction or expunge as legally invalid per chapter. (7) "Unless requested by the applicant, no hearing is necessary to grant or deny an application filed under subsection (5)." (12) Petitioning under this section does not make person ineligible to petition for misdemeanor expungement pursuant to Title 46.
  • Procedure (MT Courts / OCA instructions via courts.mt.gov + Rosebud County republication https://rosebudcountymt.gov/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/MMRTA-Instructions-for-Expungement-Petition-Forms.pdf): NO automatic expungement — petition only. Separate form per conviction, even if same time. Form A: currently serving sentence + seeking expungement, resentencing of felony as misdemeanor, or resentencing as civil infraction. Form B: sentence completed + seeking expungement, redesignation of felony as misdemeanor, or redesignation of criminal conviction as civil infraction. Petitioner needs: copy of criminal record (fee, MT DOJ https://dojmt.gov/dci-home/background-checks/); date/approx date of conviction + MT court of conviction; brief description why conviction eligible; indication whether hearing requested (entitled to hearing if wished, not required); copy of DOJ criminal record attached; service on County Attorney for county of filing + signed Certificate of Service; signature, date, printed name. Proposed order template recommended (OCA template). Petitioner responsible for filing fees + other costs, incl. directing DOJ to expunge/redesignate if successful.
  • Court structure §§16-12-114/115/116 (search-result text; official MCA pages not opened in full — Verify verbatim before final HTML): §16-12-114 defines "expungement or resentencing of marijuana conviction court" (court responsible for determining petitions under §16-12-113) + "petition for expungement or resentencing" (petition filed under §16-12-113). §16-12-115 (En. §102 Ch. 576 L. 2021): petition may be determined by judge pro tempore or special master, member of state bar, agreed in writing by petitioner + county attorney, appointed by supreme court as provided in §3-5-115, sworn to determine whether petitioner meets §16-12-113 criteria; on appointment designated decriminalized marijuana conviction expungement judge; has authority/power of elected district court judge in civil action, proceedings per district-court evidence/procedure rules, determinations same force/effect as if determined by district court with regular judge presiding; all filings filed with clerk of court in judicial district where conviction took place, copies to appointed judge. §16-12-116: when applicant requests hearing under §16-12-113, appointed judge may hear petition in any venue stipulated by petitioner + county attorney, as provided in §25-2-202, or any venue otherwise determined by judge per stipulation, taking courtroom-facility availability into consideration. Sources: https://law.justia.com/codes/montana/title-16/chapter-12/part-1/section-16-12-115/ + https://codes.findlaw.com/mt/title-16-alcohol-tobacco-and-marijuana/mt-code-ann-sect-16-12-116/ (FindLaw notes text current as of 1 Jan 2024 + subject to contingent voidness — check contingency + current status).
  • No filing deadline found in material opened — Verify whether any deadline exists in §§16-12-113–116 or court rules; do not state "no deadline" as confirmed fact without check.
  • Effect on records — partly traced, not fully traced: §16-12-113(6) expunge "as legally invalid" or redesignate. Downstream DOJ purge/sealing mechanics for granted §16-12-113 petition not fully traced. Do not conflate with §46-18-1110 / §46-32-105 certificate-of-innocence expungement, different temporary 2021 regime terminating 30 Jun 2023. Under §46-18-1110(a) (per CCResourceCenter 13 Jun 2026) granted order there directs arresting agency, prosecutor, clerk to "permanently seal" records, petitioner must send order to those agencies + DOJ with Expungement Form + fingerprint card (FD-258); DOJ shall "expunge all records of arrest, investigation, detention, and court proceedings" addressed by order — but that is §46-18-1110 mechanism, not verified §16-12-113 mechanism. Verify DOJ/record-clearance steps for granted §16-12-113 petition.
  • Case illustration (not a count): MT Supreme Court doc, State v. Maier, DA 21-0218 (23 Sep 2021), https://juddocumentservice.mt.gov/getDocByCTrackId?DocId=366549. Maier petitioned 7 Apr 2021, before HB 701 passed; State responded opposition 13 Apr 2021; district court denied; HB 701 §§41 + 48, effective 1 Jan 2021 (retroactive), superseded I-190 §§8 + 36 on which Maier relied. Opinion holding beyond retroactivity point not fully read — do not summarise holding without opening full opinion.
  • How many expungements actually occurred: NOT FOUND — Unavailable on current evidence. No official court/OCA/DOC count located; further search 1 Nov 2026 for MT marijuana expungement counts (2022–2024) returned only general state-by-state guides confirming MT petition-based, no automatic process (e.g. Marijuana Herald 14 Sep 2026, https://themarijuanaherald.com/marijuana-expungement-laws-by-state/; Asurint Clean Slate guide Dec 2025, confirming MT has no statewide automatic sealing/expungement law). Do not estimate. If no count published, final report must say so plainly.
  • What MT does NOT have on expungement: no automatic expungement under §16-12-113 (petition only); no marijuana-specific expungement beyond HB 701 §16-12-113 mechanism + general Title 46 misdemeanor-expungement route (§46-18-1101 et seq., preserved by §16-12-113(12)) located. State as finding, with §46-18-1110 distinction above.

12(c) Driving

  • Offence MCA §61-8-1002 (official MCA 2025 page opened; Run 1 secondary flag now RESOLVED): https://leg.mt.gov/bills/MCA/title_0610/chapter_0080/part_0100/section_0020/0610-0080-0100-0020.html; hist. En. §2 Ch. 498 L. 2021; amd. §2 Ch. 723 L. 2023; §7 Ch. 264 L. 2025; §3 Ch. 298 L. 2025; §1 Ch. 412 L. 2025. MT DOES have per se THC driving limit. Hypothesis MT might have no per se limit (by Wyoming comparison) NOT sustained on MT own text; Wyoming law must not be imported.
  • (1) "A person commits the offense of driving under the influence if the person drives or is in actual physical control of:" (a) vehicle/commercial motor vehicle upon ways open to public while under influence of alcohol, any drug, or combination; (b) noncommercial vehicle upon ways open to public while alcohol concentration, as shown by blood/breath/oral fluid/other bodily substance, is 0.08+; (c) commercial motor vehicle within state while alcohol 0.04+; (d) "a noncommercial vehicle or commercial motor vehicle within this state while the person's tetrahydrocannabinol level, excluding inactive metabolites, as shown by analysis of the person's blood or other bodily substance, is 5 ng/ml or more"; (e)(i) under-21 alcohol 0.02+; (e)(ii) under-21 any amount of THC, excluding inactive metabolites, in body as shown by blood/other bodily substance; (f) without valid prescription, listed prohibited substance at/above tabled blood level (amphetamine 20, cocaine 20, cocaine metabolite 20, heroin 1, morphine 20, 6-monoacetyl morphine 1, LSD 0.1, methamphetamine 20, phencyclidine 5, fentanyl 0.5 — all ng/ml).
  • Impairment vs per se: (1)(a) impairment prong; (1)(d) per se THC prong (5 ng/ml+, excluding inactive metabolites). Driver can be prosecuted on impairment evidence without 5 ng/ml result, and on 5 ng/ml result without separate impairment proof, subject to (6) (same acts may support prosecution under another subsection, but only one conviction under this section or similar previous-law offense). (2) Alcohol inferences only: ≤0.04 may be inferred not under influence of alcohol; >0.04 <0.08 no inference but may be considered with other competent evidence; ≥0.08 rebuttable inference under influence of alcohol. No parallel THC inference table in (2). (3) (2) does not limit other competent evidence on under influence of alcohol/drugs/combination. (4) Each municipality may enact this section as ordinance (word "state" changed to "municipality") + enforce. (5) Absolute liability, as provided in §45-2-104, imposed for violation.
  • No medical-cardholder/patient exception appears in §61-8-1002 as opened. Whether any exception exists elsewhere (e.g. §61-8-1001 definitions, or prescription defence under (1)(f) for non-THC substances) not confirmed — Verify; do not state patient exception exists or does not exist beyond §61-8-1002 text. Note: §61-8-1002(1)(f) expressly "without a valid prescription," but (1)(d) (THC) contains no prescription qualifier on its face.
  • "Under the influence" definition — Verify verbatim: §61-8-1001(14) "'Under the influence' has the meaning provided in 61-1-101." §61-1-101 text not opened. Secondary (NCDD; DrivingLaws.org, prior round) cite as: as result of taking alcohol/drugs/combination into body, person's ability to safely operate vehicle has been diminished. Do not quote as verbatim until §61-1-101 opened.
  • "Drug" definition §61-8-1001(7) (official MCA 2025 page opened; En. §1 Ch. 498 L. 2021; amd. §44 Ch. 47 L. 2025; §6 Ch. 264 L. 2025; §2 Ch. 298 L. 2025): https://leg.mt.gov/bills/MCA/title_0610/chapter_0080/part_0100/section_0010/0610-0080-0100-0010.html — "'Drug' means any substance that when taken into the human body can impair a person's ability to operate a vehicle safely. The term includes the meanings provided in 50-32-101(6), (7), and (14)."
  • Aggravated DUI definition §61-8-1001(1) (same page): "Aggravated driving under the influence" means person in violation of §61-8-1002(1)(a)/(b)/(c)/(d)/(f) AND: (a) alcohol concentration 0.16+; (b) under court/department order to equip any vehicle operated with approved ignition interlock device; (c) driver's licence/privilege suspended, canceled, or revoked as result of prior DUI violation (incl. (1)(a)–(d)/(f), aggravated DUI, or similar other-state/previous-law offense); or (d) refuses breath sample as required in §61-8-1016 + licence/privilege suspended/canceled/revoked under implied-consent statute. Consequence for THC: THC-only per se DUI can be charged as aggravated only via (b)/(c)/(d) (interlock order, prior-suspension status, or implied-consent refusal + suspension) — not via (a), alcohol-based. Do not apply 0.16 alcohol aggravator to THC.
  • "Passenger area" definition §61-8-1001(13) (same page): "the area designed to seat the driver and passengers while a motor vehicle is in operation and any area that is readily accessible to the driver or a passenger while the driver or a passenger is seated in the vehicle, including an unlocked glove compartment." Also governs §61-8-1027 below.
  • Penalties MCA §61-8-1007 first–third offenses (official MCA 2025 opened; En. §3 Ch. 498 L. 2021; amd. §4 Ch. 226 L. 2025; §8 Ch. 264 L. 2025): https://leg.mt.gov/bills/MCA/title_0610/chapter_0080/part_0100/section_0070/0610-0080-0100-0070.html
  • (1)(a) Impairment-based DUI (§61-8-1002(1)(a)): first — imprisonment ≥24 consecutive hrs ≤6 months, fine $600–$1,000 (passenger under 16: 48 hrs–1 yr, $1,200–$2,000); second — 7 days–1 yr, $1,200–$2,000 (under-16 passenger: 14 days–1 yr, $2,400–$4,000); third — 30 days–1 yr, $2,500–$5,000 (under-16 passenger: 60 days–1 yr, $5,000–$10,000). (1)(b) If prior conviction under §45-5-106 or §45-5-107, punishment as §61-8-1008. (1)(c) If prior conviction or pending charge for DUI (incl. (1)(a)–(d)) or similar offense meeting aggravated-DUI definition, punishment as (4) (aggravated tier). (1)(d)–(e) Mandatory minimum imprisonment may not be served under home arrest + may not be suspended unless judge finds imposition would pose risk to person's physical/mental well-being; remainder may be suspended up to 1 yr pending successful completion of chemical dependency treatment programme under §61-8-1009, with DUI-court / 24/7 sobriety + drug monitoring conditions if available/imposed, remainder imposable on violation. (2)(a) Per se DUI (§61-8-1002(1)(b)/(c)/(d)/(f)) — tier a 5 ng/ml THC DUI falls in: first — imprisonment ≤6 months (no minimum stated), fine $600–$1,000 (under-16 passenger: ≤6 months, $1,200–$2,000); second — 5 days–1 yr, $1,200–$2,000 (under-16 passenger: 10 days–1 yr, $2,400–$4,000); third — 30 days–1 yr, $2,500–$5,000 (under-16 passenger: 60 days–1 yr, $5,000–$10,000). (2)(b)–(e) mirror (1)(b)–(e) for priors/aggravated/suspension. (3)(a) Under-21 DUI (§61-8-1002(1)(e)): first — fine $100–$500; second — fine $200–$500 + if 18+ incarceration ≤10 days; third/subsequent — fine $300–$500 + if 18+ incarceration ≥24 consecutive hrs ≤60 days. (3)(a)(iv) In addition, regardless of disposition: chemical dependency education/treatment under §61-8-1009 as ordered; department shall suspend driver's licence 90 days first conviction, 6 months second, 1 yr third/subsequent; no restricted/probationary licence during suspension until reinstatement fee under §61-2-107 paid + if under 18 at offense, at least 30 days suspension completed. (3)(b) Conviction under this subsection may not be counted as prior offense/conviction under §§61-8-1007/1008/1011. Caution: 90-day/6-month/1-yr licence figures in secondary summaries are this under-21 schedule; do not present as adult DUI licence schedule. (4)(a) Aggravated DUI: first — imprisonment ≥2 days ≤1 yr, fine $1,000 (under-16 passenger: 4 consecutive days–1 yr, $2,000); second — 15 days–1 yr, $2,500 (under-16 passenger: 45 days–1 yr, $5,000); third — 40 consecutive days–1 yr, $5,000 (under-16 passenger: 90 consecutive days–1 yr, $10,000). (4)(b)–(c) mirror home-arrest/suspension limits + §45-5-106/107 → §61-8-1008 rule. (5) In addition to punishment, regardless of disposition, chemical dependency education/treatment under §61-8-1009 as ordered. (6) Person punished under this section subject to mandatory revocation/suspension of driver's licence as provided in chapter 5.
  • Licence consequences for adult DUI — Verify: §61-8-1007(6) cross-refers to Title 61 Ch. 5, but Ch. 5 revocation/suspension periods for adult first/second/third DUI not opened. Do not state adult licence periods without opening §§61-5-205/61-5-208 or applicable Ch. 5 section. Implied-consent (§§61-8-1005 et seq., incl. §61-8-1016 breath-sample refusal referenced in aggravated definition) likewise not opened — Verify before stating refusal consequences.
  • Prior-conviction lookback — Verify: no lookback period (e.g. 10 years) appears in §61-8-1007 text opened; secondary asserting 10-year lookback for second/third counting, or lifetime counting for third-subsequent, not confirmed vs §61-8-1011 or §61-8-1008, must not be stated without opening those sections.
  • Fourth+ offenses MCA §61-8-1008 (official MCA 2025 opened; En. §4 Ch. 498 L. 2021; amd. §2 Ch. 473 L. 2021; §5 Ch. 226 L. 2025; §9 Ch. 264 L. 2025): https://leg.mt.gov/bills/MCA/title_0610/chapter_0080/part_0100/section_0080/0610-0080-0100-0080.html
  • (1)(a) Person convicted of DUI (incl. §61-8-1002(1)(a)–(d)/(f), aggravated DUI, or similar previous/other-state offense) who also convicted under §45-5-106 or §45-5-107, or any combination of 3+ convictions under §§45-5-104, 45-5-205, 45-5-628(1)(e), DUI, aggravated DUI, or similar offenses (with §45-5-104 offense occurring while operating under influence per §61-8-1002(1)(a)), guilty of felony, punished by: (i) DOC sentence ≥13 months ≤2 yrs for placement in appropriate correctional facility/programme, followed by consecutive 5-yr term to MT state prison or MT women's prison, all suspended, + fine $5,000–$10,000; or (ii) up to 5 yrs in appropriate treatment court programme, with required completion, + fine $5,000–$10,000 (suspended sentence possible, deferred imposition not eligible). (1)(b) Under (1)(a)(i): imposition/execution may not be deferred/suspended, no parole eligibility; programme may be DOC-approved residential alcohol treatment programme; successful completion converts remainder of 13-month–2-yr term to probation with standard DOC probation conditions, cost payment if financially able, no frequenting alcohol-serving establishments, no alcohol consumption, no driving unless authorised by probation officer, aftercare for probationary period, random/routine drug + alcohol testing, ignition interlock if permitted to drive. (2) Person with prior §45-5-106 conviction or 4+ prior convictions (same combination) previously sentenced under (1)(a)(i) or (1)(a)(ii): fine $5,000–$10,000 + state prison ≤10 yrs; no deferred imposition. (3) If previously convicted + sentenced under (2): fine $5,000–$10,000 + state prison ≤25 yrs; no deferred imposition. (4) If presently being sentenced + previously convicted/sentenced under (3) ≥1 time: fine $5,000–$10,000 + state prison ≥5 yrs ≤25 yrs; first 5 yrs may not be suspended.
