(6) Mandatory revocation/suspension as provided in chapter 5 (same Ch. 5 Verify as above).
  • Secondary conflict resolved: LegalClarity "up to 25 years" for fourth offense matches §61-8-1008(3) (after (2) sentence), not first fourth-offense sentence under (1)(a)(i) (13 months–2 yrs + 5 yrs suspended). Older summaries "13 months to 5 years" conflate (1) + (2). Cite tier structure, not single fourth-offense maximum.
  • 2025 DUI bills (audit correction): HB 344 (2025) WAS enacted (enrolled bill; chapter assigned 5 May 2025) and is the source of the §61-8-1002(1)(f) prohibited-substance prong (confirm the Ch. 264 / 298 / 412 mapping on the leg.mt.gov bill page); HB 541 (2025) DIED in Senate committee 22 May 2025 and never became law. Neither is merely “pending”. §61-8-1002 history already shows 2025 amendments (Ch. 264, 298, 412 L. 2025) + §61-8-1007 shows Ch. 226 + 264 L. 2025, so some 2025 DUI change enacted; which bill produced which chapter is Verify.
  • Vehicle storage / open-container analogue — RESOLVED in Title 61 (Run 1 gap closed). Official MCA 2025 page opened: https://leg.mt.gov/bills/MCA/title_0610/chapter_0080/part_0100/section_0270/0610-0080-0100-0270.html; hist. En. §8 Ch. 576 L. 2021 (HB 701).
  • §61-8-1027 Unlawful possession of marijuana, marijuana products, or marijuana paraphernalia in motor vehicle on highway: (1) "Except as provided in subsection (2), a person commits the offense of unlawful possession of marijuana, marijuana products, or marijuana paraphernalia in a motor vehicle if the person knowingly possesses marijuana, marijuana products, or marijuana paraphernalia, as those terms are defined in 16-12-102, within the passenger area of a motor vehicle on a highway." (2) Does not apply to marijuana/products/paraphernalia: (a) purchased from dispensary + remaining in unopened, original packaging; (b) in locked glove compartment or storage compartment; (c) in motor vehicle trunk or luggage compartment or truck bed or cargo compartment; (d) behind last upright seat of motor vehicle not equipped with trunk; or (e) in closed container in area of motor vehicle not equipped with trunk + not normally occupied by driver/passenger. (3)(a) Conviction: fine ≤$100. (3)(b) Violation is not criminal offense within §§3-1-317, 3-1-318, 45-2-101, 46-18-236, 61-8-104, or 61-8-711 + may not be recorded/charged against driver's record, + insurance company may not hold violation against insured or increase premiums because of it. Surcharges under §§3-1-317, 3-1-318, 46-18-236 may not be imposed.
  • Corroborating DOR guidance (opened 1 Nov 2026): https://revenue.mt.gov/card/cannabis/education/know-before-you-go — "Know Before You Go" confirms same storage list, adds insurance point, states crossing state lines in motor vehicle with personal adult-use or medicinal marijuana/products/paraphernalia prohibited under any circumstances, no exception for medicinal marijuana.
  • Correction to Run 1 framing: Run 1 correctly stated no such rule located in §§16-12-106/108. Rule exists, but in Title 61 (§61-8-1027, HB 701), not Ch. 12. Present as Title 61 rule. Note no separate MT "open container" statute for marijuana analogous to alcohol open-container definition (§61-8-1001(12), quoted above for alcohol only); §61-8-1027 is marijuana vehicle-possession rule, turns on passenger-area possession, not "open container" concept.

12(d) Firearms — federal §922(g)(3) conflict, post-Schedule III question UNRESOLVED

Do not give definitive legal conclusion in final report. Position: statute + binding circuit law + secondary claims of pending ATF change, each labelled.

  • Federal statute (secondary summary, Ammo.com, last updated 14 Jul 2026, https://ammo.com/articles/protection-pain-treatment-choosing-between-guns-medical-marijuana): 18 U.S.C. §922(g)(3), originating 1968 Gun Control Act, prohibits anyone who unlawfully uses controlled substance, as defined by CSA, from owning firearms. Not marijuana-specific; covers every controlled substance. ATF Form 4473 historically carried warning marijuana use federally illegal even for medical purposes, preventing medically licensed patient from purchasing/owning via FFL.
  • Binding circuit law for MT (same secondary source): 2016, 9th Circuit ruled in Wilson v. Lynch FFL dealers bound by Form 4473 question 21f, under which unlawful drug users prohibited from owning firearms + dealers therefore could not sell to them. Wilson v. Lynch, 835 F.3d 1083 (9th Cir. 2016), upheld under pre-Bruen intermediate scrutiny the dealer-sale denial / ATF Open Letter challenge; the plaintiff had not alleged unlawful use or firearm possession, so the court did not decide a possession/owning challenge. [AUDIT: do not state Wilson decided possession, and do not state it remains settled law after Bruen and any 2026 developments without re-checking.] Verify Wilson v. Lynch, 835 F.3d 1083 (9th Cir. 2016), + whether remains good law after 2026 developments below, before stating as current binding law.
  • Post-Schedule III secondary claims — Verify vs ATF/DOJ primary sources; do not present as settled law: MT radio-site articles (K99 Hits / 560 KMON / 945 Max Country, same syndicated text, last updated ~172 days before access) state Apr 2026 DOJ/DEA moved FDA-approved + qualifying state-regulated medical marijuana to Schedule III, ATF published proposed new Form 4473 wording omitting previous medical-marijuana warning, public comments taken until 7 Jul 2026, ATF would review comments + decide whether to allow ownership later in 2026. Sources: https://k99hits.com/ixp/1107/p/medical-marijuana-gun-rights/ ; https://560kmon.com/ixp/1107/p/medical-marijuana-gun-rights/. Local-news rewrites, not ATF proposal itself; final Form 4473 text + any ATF guidance as of 1 Nov 2026 not verified.
  • Further secondary claim — Verify, do not rely on: Ammo.com also asserts unanimous (9-0) Supreme Court ruling in 2026 in favour of young man prosecuted for owning pistol while admitting occasional marijuana use (cited as "U.S. v. Hemani 2026") + states medical users remain legal grey area with ATF guidance yet to come. No Supreme Court opinion opened to verify case name/holding/scope. Do not cite Hemani as authority without opening opinion.
  • Unresolved legal question to flag verbatim in final report: after 28 Apr 2026 Schedule III move for state-licensed medical marijuana, is MT medical cardholder's use still "unlawful use" under §922(g)(3)? Arguments both ways + no MT court/agency guidance resolving located: (i) Schedule III substances still require lawful prescription/dispensing channel under federal law, state medical cards are not federal prescriptions, which would leave use "unlawful" for §922(g)(3) purposes; (ii) Apr 2026 order's own federal-registration framework for qualifying medical dispensaries (see secondary note below) suggests qualifying medical activity no longer treated as Schedule I unlawful activity, but framework addresses dispensary registration, not individual firearm possession. State question, state no MT guidance located, give no answer.
  • MT state firearms-law interaction: none located. No MT statute found separately prohibiting/protecting firearm possession by marijuana users, no MT court/agency guidance on §922(g)(3) question located. Do not invent MT firearms provision. If parent needs MT-law firearms section, must be researched as separate Title 45 Ch. 8 / Art. II §12 question + flagged Unavailable for now.
  • Secondary federal-registration note (context only, not MT law): DOJ announced 30 Oct 2026 DEA issued first federal registrations to 900+ medical marijuana dispensaries in 7 states (Alabama, Florida, Mississippi, South Dakota, Texas, Virginia, West Virginia), state licensure alone does not bring federal registration (each dispensary passed DEA pre-registration investigation), recreational marijuana remains Schedule I. Sources (secondary): https://www.thedigitalfreepress.com/post/dea-first-federal-registrations-medical-marijuana-dispensaries-seven-states ; https://themarijuanaherald.com/2026/10/dea-grants-federal-registrations-to-more-than-900-medical-marijuana-dispensaries-across-seven-states/ (published 30 Oct 2026). Montana not in first group per those reports, no MT dispensary federal registration verified. Verify vs DOJ release (justice.gov/opa) before citing.

12(e) Other consequences (cross-reference summary — full verbatim in Run 1 file)

  • Custody: §16-12-106(8)–(9) protections (Run 1 anchor). Full verbatim in Run 1 file; this round did not re-open §16-12-106 in full, does not re-quote. Do not expand protection beyond Run 1 without re-opening section.
  • Professional licensing: §16-12-515(2) (Run 1 anchor). Same caveat: cross-reference only.
  • Employment (beyond Part 11): tension §16-12-108(5) (no accommodation required; discipline for workplace drug-policy violation or working while intoxicated allowed; no wrongful-discharge/discrimination cause of action — Run 1 anchor) vs §39-2-313 (HB 655, 2021 — now fully verified this round, see below). Do not duplicate Part 11 discussion at length; cross-refer.
  • §39-2-313 Discrimination prohibited for use of lawful product during nonworking hours — exceptions (official MCA 2025 page opened; En. §1 Ch. 193 L. 1993; amd. §3 Ch. 315 L. 2011; §66 Ch. 576 L. 2021 HB 701). Resolves Run 1 "full verbatim Verify" for §39-2-313. Source: https://leg.mt.gov/bills/MCA/title_0390/chapter_0020/part_0030/section_0130/0390-0020-0030-0130.html
  • (1) "'lawful product' means a product that is legally consumed, used, or enjoyed and includes food, beverages, tobacco, and marijuana." (2) "Except as provided in subsections (3) and (4), an employer may not refuse to employ or license and may not discriminate against an individual with respect to compensation, promotion, or the terms, conditions, or privileges of employment because the individual legally uses a lawful product off the employer's premises during nonworking hours." (3) (2) does not apply to: (a) use that (i) affects in any manner individual's ability to perform job-related responsibilities or safety of other employees, or (ii) conflicts with bona fide occupational qualification reasonably related to employment; (b) individual who, on personal basis, has professional service contract with employer + unique nature of services authorises employer, as part of contract, to limit use of certain products; or (c) nonprofit employer whose primary purpose/objective is discouraging use of ≥1 lawful products by general public. (4) Employer does not violate this section if it takes action based on belief its actions permissible under established substance abuse/alcohol programme/policy, professional contract, or collective bargaining agreement. (5) Employer may offer/impose health/disability/life insurance policy making distinctions by type/price of coverage based on product use if: (a) differential rates reflect actuarially justified differences; (b) employee receives written notice delineating differential rates used by carriers; (c) distinctions are not used to expand/limit/curtail rights/liabilities in civil cause of action.
  • Tension to state plainly: §39-2-313 protects off-premises, non-working-hours lawful use (incl. marijuana as "lawful product"), subject to (3)/(4) exceptions, while §16-12-108(5) says Ch. 12 does not require accommodation + does not create wrongful-discharge/discrimination cause of action for discipline for workplace drug-policy violation or working while intoxicated. Two provisions never reconciled in MT appellate decision located this round — Verify case law before stating which provision prevails in any given employment dispute, + note safety-sensitive/DOT/impairment exceptions in brief are reflected in §39-2-313(3)(a) (ability/safety, bona fide occupational qualification) + §16-12-108(5), not as separate DOT statute opened here.
  • Housing: no MT statute located separately protecting/prohibiting marijuana users in housing. Do not state housing protection or ban. Federal public-housing / Section 8 consequences are federal law, not MT law, not researched — Unavailable for this report.
  • Public benefits: no MT statute located creating marijuana-specific public-benefits disqualification/protection. Do not state one. Federal benefits consequences (e.g. SNAP/TANF drug-felony rules, turn on federal/state opt-out law) not researched — Unavailable.
  • Immigration (federal only, brief, flagged federal not MT law): federal immigration consequences of marijuana conduct (inadmissibility, deportability, good-moral-character bars) governed by federal law (INA), not researched; MT legalisation does not control federal immigration outcomes. Do not state any immigration consequence beyond flag without federal-law research.

12(f) What Montana does NOT have — stated plainly

  1. MT DOES have per se THC driving limit (5 ng/ml, excluding inactive metabolites, §61-8-1002(1)(d)), + impairment prong (§61-8-1002(1)(a)), + zero-tolerance THC prong for under-21 drivers (§61-8-1002(1)(e)(ii)). "No per se limit" hypothesis false for MT. Do not use Wyoming comparison as MT law.
  2. No automatic marijuana expungement. §16-12-113 petition-only; no marijuana-specific expungement beyond HB 701 §16-12-113 mechanism (+ general Title 46 route preserved by §16-12-113(12)) located.
  3. No standalone public enforcement/disciplinary list found in Run 1 or this round.
  4. No hemp-specific security rule located (see 11(b)).
  5. No separate adult-use per-day purchase cap in §16-12-224; adult-use transaction test is possession-amount test in §16-12-224(9) (see 11(c)).
  6. No MT state firearms provision on marijuana users located (see 12(d)).
  7. No inspection-frequency rule located in texts opened (see 11(d)).
  8. No MT marijuana-specific housing, public-benefits, or immigration provision located (see 12(e)).

COMBINED RESEARCH NOTES — RUNS 1 AND 2

These notes are part of the deliverable. They record what was not settled, what was corrected on merging, and what conflicts remain, so no Verify item is silently presented as fact. "Verify" means: open the named official source and confirm before relying. "Unavailable" means: no official source was located by the route tried, which is stated so the gap can be retried rather than silently dropped. Proposed, vetoed, failed and draft measures are labelled as such and must not be quoted as current law. County and city zoning entries dated 2021–2025 must be re-pulled from the current city/county code before any site or licensing decision.

Scope, cut-off and audit limits

Research and access date throughout: 1 November 2026. The 3 November 2026 election had not been held at that date; no 2026 election result (including any Montana ballot outcome) is stated in this file. Federal hemp dates 12 November and 11 December 2026, and the USDA DEA-registered-lab enforcement date 31 December 2026, fall after the cut-off; no Montana agency response to the federal hemp dates was located as of 1 November 2026. Within Run 1, the following were not independently re-verified against live primary text in that run and are flagged where they appear: full MCA Part 5 (§§16-12-501–514), full §16-12-301, full §§15-64-101/102, §50-32-222, §39-2-313 (now verified verbatim in Part 12), §61-8-1002 (now opened in Part 12), and ARM texts retrieved via mirrors. Secondary sources were used only for non-law facts or where an official page could not be rendered as text, and are named as secondary where they are used. A fact flagged Verify is not a confirmed fact; a fact flagged Unavailable was not found, which is not proof that it does not exist.

Reconciliations applied on merging (items 1–20) — see also the cover and the prefatory box before Part 0

  1. Original green count: 28 Yes counties in the 2020 I-190 SOS canvass (Yes 341,037 / 56.90%, No 258,337 / 43.10%); current count 29 green / 27 red (Dawson in December 2021, Granite out June 2022, Custer in November 2024). Any Run 1 wording presenting 29 as the 2020 result is corrected by this note and by §9.4.
  2. Combined-use §16-12-225: maximum 8, one per tribe or majority-tribal-owned entity, "one cultivator license and one dispensary license", fee $7,500, county §16-12-301 condition only if the county voted against I-190. No tier-1, no same-premises, no 150-air-mile wording in the current text; CCD FAQ wording to that effect is stale; DOR 2023 summary says HB 128 removed share-location and proximity limits. Parts 0 and 4 are corrected by this note and by Part 10 §10.2.
  3. MDA hemp chapter: Title 80, Chapter 18 (§§80-18-101–111), corroborated by the MCA 2025 index, MDA pages and the State Hemp Plan — the Chapter 27 citation was not corroborated anywhere opened. Chapter 18 is used everywhere in this file.
  4. MAR 2026-155.2 effective 26 September 2026 (ARM 42.39.102/105, day-to-day operational control and residency, change-report 10 days to 30 days). The Part 9–10 caveat (adoption notice not opened in that track, V-15) is stated alongside the Part 11 finding that the adoption notice was opened; neither finding is overclaimed.
  5. Delivery: transporter delivery is cardholder-only (ARM 42.39.413, MCA §16-12-222(7)); adult-use consumer delivery is prohibited. Dispensary-own delivery remains Verify beyond the April 2022 newsletter hand-delivery exception for a cardholder in a vehicle parked immediately outside the dispensary.
  6. DUI: Montana has a per se THC limit — §61-8-1002(1)(d), 5 ng/mL or more, excluding inactive metabolites — plus the impairment prong (1)(a) and the under-21 zero-tolerance THC prong (1)(e)(ii). Penalties per §61-8-1007 (per se tier (2)) and the §61-8-1008 tiered fourth-plus structure; cite tiers, not single-figure secondary maxima. Adult licence periods under Title 61 Chapter 5 remain Verify (the 90-day / 6-month / 1-year figures are the under-21 schedule in §61-8-1007(3)).
  7. Vehicle storage: §61-8-1027 (Title 61, HB 701) — passenger-area possession offence, fine up to $100, not criminal, not on the driver record, with the (2) exceptions (unopened original packaging; locked glove or storage compartment; trunk, luggage, bed or cargo compartment; behind the last upright seat; closed container in an area not normally occupied). Run 1's "not located in §§16-12-106/108" was correct as to those sections; §61-8-1027 is presented as the Title 61 rule.
  8. Under-21 marijuana offence: §45-5-639 (current MCA 2025, En. §2, Ch. 155, L. 2025) — older §45-5-624 citations are alcohol-code numbering, superseded or confused for marijuana.
  9. Criminal: §45-9-102 current text — state prison up to 5 years, fine up to $5,000, no 60-gram tier (pre-2021 figures superseded; NORML $45,000/$50,000 figures conflict and are flagged, not adopted). §45-9-110(3) marijuana/THC tier: up to 5 years / $5,000, except more than 1 lb or more than 30 plants: up to 25 years / $50,000; weight means dry plant including leaves and stem, excluding roots. §50-32-222(4)(ff) delta-8 is expressly Schedule I; (4)(kk) synthetic cannabinoids; (4)(x) marijuana is Schedule I subject to the "unless specifically excepted" headnote.
  10. Edible serving and transaction test: 5 mg (MCA 2025 §16-12-224(8)(b)(iv), HB 636 effective 1 July 2026) — 10 mg in 2021 text is superseded. Adult-use transaction test: §16-12-224(9) possession-amount test — no separate adult-use per-day cap; medical up to 1 oz per day and 5 oz per month (§16-12-515) is a Part 5 medical rule, and "5 oz possession" is wrong.
  11. Employment: §39-2-313 full verbatim now verified (lawful product including marijuana; off-premises, non-working-hours protection subject to (3)/(4) exceptions) versus §16-12-108(5) (no accommodation required; discipline for a workplace policy violation or working while intoxicated allowed; no wrongful-discharge or discrimination cause of action) — the tension is stated plainly; no Montana appellate reconciliation was located.
  12. Testing: §16-12-209 duty, §16-12-206 lab and Scientific Director qualifications, ARM 42.39.602/603/610/614 (Cornell mirrors — rules.mt.gov re-check required). Moisture conflict unresolved: 15.0% (Cornell page opened) versus 12.0% (Esper snippet) — both are presented, neither is picked silently. QA Appendix Tables and SOP-001 numeric batch sizes were not obtained — they are not stated. MAR 2025-372.1 (batch-size increase) and MAR 2026-129.1 (video-format amendment) are proposed, not law. ARM 42.39.122 as opened shows only 2021 NEW history — any 2026 amendment is Verify.
  13. Part 7 findings carried in full: two-stage grower licensing (Stage 1 Planting Permit to Stage 2 Production licence on Planting Report), valid to 31 December 2026, non-transferable, no planting until the permit issues; fees $850 (A/B-only) / $1,100 (Category C) plus $250 extra samples/inspections (the ARM flat $1,100 versus the MDA $850/$1,100 split is flagged as Verify in Part 7); 10-year felony bar and FBI Identity History Summary for key participants; separate research licence in addition; testing resolved for official testing: post-decarboxylation LC method, Total THC = Δ9-THC + (THCA × 0.8772), official measurement uncertainty 0.0453%, MSU Analytical Lab (DEA RS0591506 per the 2023 Plan); sampling 100% Category C / 20% B / 10% A, producers may not sample their own crops; 3 negligent violations in 5 years lead to 5-year ineligibility; remediation capped below 1.0% (below 2.0% fibre-only), extraction and dilution no longer allowed; processor Part A $2,500 per year / Part B $1,000 per year, 1-mile radius per-location rule, own-production exemption (2026 processor form is Verify — only the 2025 form was located; live seed-provider list names are Verify — only a stale 1/10/2022 copy was retrieved; whether a newer State Hemp Plan exists after 3 January 2023 is Verify).

Crow, Northern Cheyenne and Little Shell are not listed — marked Unavailable, not "no programme".

Consolidated unresolved conflicts (all runs)

  • ARM 42.39.614 moisture failure: 15.0% (Cornell page opened) versus 12.0% (Esper staging-mirror snippet) — check the current rules.mt.gov text.
  • §45-9-102 fine cap: $5,000 (current MCA 2025 text opened) versus $45,000/$50,000 in NORML and pre-2021 secondary tables; no 60-gram tier in the current text.
  • Edible serving: 5 mg (MCA 2025 §16-12-224(8)(b)(iv), HB 636) versus 10 mg in 2021 Justia text — 5 mg is current.
  • Fourth-offence DUI maximum: tiered structure in §61-8-1008(1)–(4) versus single-figure secondary summaries ("up to 25 years" / "13 months to 5 years") — cite tiers.
  • Under-21 offence numbering: §45-5-639 (current MCA 2025 page opened) versus §45-5-624 in older summaries — §45-5-639 is the marijuana provision.
  • Vehicle-storage rule location: Title 61, §61-8-1027 (HB 701), not §§16-12-106/108 — Run 1's "not located in §§16-12-106/108" was correct as to those sections.
  • DPHHS versus DOR testing oversight: DOR/CARD is current (HB 701 transfer); FoodHandlersGuide DPHHS material is stale and its numeric limits must not be quoted.
  • Broughton: Building Department "approved and ready to issue" email (23 April 2025) versus Butte-Silver Bow's subsequent §16-12-207 same-street / 500-foot refusal — both facts are in the order excerpt; the order resolves the dispute in Butte-Silver Bow's favour on the excerpt's framing, but the full holding was not read.
  • All Run 1 and Run 2 conflicts listed in the sections above (cardholders, dispensary counts, lab counts and names, 2025 tax, manufacturer fee basis, medical conversion cutoff, hemp 2021 acreage scope, MSU Bozeman poll sample, SB 537 versus SB 442, hemp plan dates, HB 701 sign date, and CannBus licence-count figures) remain open except where a reconciliation item above expressly resolves them.
